Exact dg categories II : The Embedding Theorem
Xiaofa Chen

Abstract
. For an exact dg category 𝒜 \mathcal{A} , we introduce its bounded dg derived category 𝒟 d ​ g b ​ ( 𝒜 ) \mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}) and establish the universal exact morphism from 𝒜 \mathcal{A} to 𝒟 d ​ g b ​ ( 𝒜 ) \mathcal{D}^{b}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}) . We prove that the dg quotient of an exact dg category by a subcategory of projective-injectives carries a canonical exact structure. We show that exact dg categories reproduce under tensor products and functor dg categories. We apply our results to 0-Auslander extriangulated categories and confirm a conjecture by Fang–Gorsky–Palu–Plamondon–Pressland for the algebraic case.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
18G35, 18G25, 18E20, 16E30, 16E45

1.
Introduction

In
[
9
]
the notion of exact dg category is proposed.
Roughly speaking, an exact structure on an additive dg category
\mathcal{A}

is a class
\mathcal{S}
of homotopy short exact sequences in
\mathcal{A}
, satisfying certain axioms analogous
to those of Quillen.
For exact dg categories
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
,
a morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
in the localisation
\mathrm{Hqe}
of the category of dg categories with respect to quasi-equivalences is exact if it sends the objects in
\mathcal{S}
to objects in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.
The subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}
consisting of exact dg categories with exact morphisms is denoted by
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}
.
The main motivation is to provide dg enhancements for Nakaoka–Palu’s extriangulated categories.
One of the main results in
[
9
]
is that for an exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
, the category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
carries a canonical extriangulated structure
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
, cf.
[
9
, Theorem 4.26]
.
In analogy with the notion of algebraic triangulated category
[
25
]
, an extriangulated category of this form will be called
algebraic
.

In this paper, we prove several theorems concerning exact dg categories.
Our first result relates exact dg categories to pretriangulated dg categories.

Theorem A

(Theorem
3.1
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a small exact dg category.
There exists a universal exact morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})

in
\mathrm{Hqe}
from
\mathcal{A}
to a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
If moreover
\mathcal{A}
is connective, this morphism satisfies:

1)

It induces a quasi-equivalence from
\tau_{\leq 0}{\mathcal{A}}
to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
for an extension-closed dg subcategory
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
;

2)

It induces a natural bijection
\mathbb{E}(C,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(FC,FA)
for each pair of objects
C,A
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
where
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})=H^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
.

We call
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
the bounded dg derived category of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

We construct
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
as the dg quotient
of the pretriangulated hull
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
by a full dg subcategory
\mathcal{N}
, which generalises the dg category of acyclic
complexes over a Quillen exact category.
As a consequence, for a connective exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
, the morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
induces a canonical isomorphism
K_{0}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}K_{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
between Grothendieck groups, cf. Proposition
3.5
.
Based on Theorem
A
, we obtain a characterisation of algebraic extriangulated categories, cf. Definition-Proposition
3.14
.
Using a general result concerning
\delta
-functors with weakly effaceable bimodules, cf. Corollary
2.6
, we obtain that the morphism
F
also induces natural bijections between higher extension groups, cf. Proposition
3.17
.

For an extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}
with a subcategory
\mathcal{P}
consisting of (not necessarily all) projective-injective objects in
\mathcal{C}
,
Nakaoka–Palu showed that the ideal quotient
\mathcal{C}/[\mathcal{P}]
has the structure of an extriangulated category,
induced from that of
\mathcal{C}
, cf.
[
31
, Proposition 3.30]
.
Our second result enhances theirs.

Theorem B

(Theorem
3.23
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a small connective exact dg category and
\mathcal{P}
a full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
consisting of projective-injective objects in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the canonical dg enhancement of
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Then the dg quotient
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
carries a canonical exact dg structure
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
induced from that of
\mathcal{A}

and its dg derived category is quasi-equivalent to
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.

As a consequence, we obtain that for a Quillen exact category
\mathcal{E}
and a full subcategory
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of projective-injectives in
\mathcal{E}
,
the dg quotient
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{P}_{0}
carries a canonical exact structure.
In
[
10
, 7.3.15]
, it is proven that every
\infty
-category is equivalent to the localization of the nerve of a small category by a subcategory.
We have an analogous result in the context of exact dg categories: each connective exact dg category is exactly quasi-equivalent to
the dg quotient
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{P}_{0}
of a Quillen exact category
\mathcal{E}
by a full dg subcategory
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of projective-injectives in
\mathcal{E}
, cf. Corollary
3.25
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
be small exact dg categories.
Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime})
be another small exact dg category.
A morphism
\mu:\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
is
biexact
, if for each object
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
B\in\mathcal{B}
, the induced morphisms

\mu_{A,-}:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{C},\;\;\mu_{-,B}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}

are both exact.
A famous slogan
[
32
, Remark 16]
by Amnon Neeman is: triangulated categories do not reproduce.
Our third result reveals a key feature of exact dg categories.

Theorem C

(Theorem
4.1
, Proposition
4.9
)
.

Exact dg categories do reproduce under tensor products and functor dg categories.
More precisely:

1)

If
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is connective, then
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
carries a canonical exact structure.

2)

If both
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
are connective, then there exists the universal biexact morphism
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B}
.

Let
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}
consisting of connective exact dg categories.
Building upon Theorem
C
, we show that a certain monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
is closed, cf. Corollary
4.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be an extriangulated category.
We denote by
\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}
respectively
\mathrm{inj}\,\mathcal{C}
its full subcategory of projectives respectively injectives.
If the extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}
is algebraic, then the ideal quotient
\mathcal{C}/[\mathrm{inj}\,\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}]

is also algebraic, cf. Proposition
3.16
.
When
\mathcal{C}
is furthermore
0
-Auslander
in the sense of
[
16
]
,
our last result confirms a conjecture by Fang–Gorsky–Palu–Plamondon–Pressland for
the algebraic case.

Theorem D

(Theorem
5.7
)
.

For each algebraic
0
-Auslander extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}
, we have
an equivalence of extriangulated categories

\mathcal{C}/[\mathrm{inj}\,\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}]\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{H}^{[-1,0]}(\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}/[\mathcal{I}])

where
\mathcal{I}
is the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
consisting of projective-injectives.

The key ingredient in the proof of Theorem
D
is the
0
-Auslander correspondence between the equivalence classes of certain pairs of connective dg categories and the equivalence classes of
0
-Auslander exact dg categories, cf. Theorem
5.1
.

The paper is structured as follows.
In Section
2
, we collect basic results about extriangulated categories,
and some basic notations and terminology needed in this paper.
We prove a general result concerning
\delta
-functors with weakly effaceable bimodules, cf. Corollary
2.6
.
It is a key ingredient in comparing higher extension groups in Proposition
3.17
.

In Section
3
, we prove the embedding theorem for exact dg categories.
We employ it to provide a characterisation of algebraic extriangulated categories, cf. Proposition-Definition
3.14
.
Additionally, we study the relation between higher extensions.
Furthermore, we study the dg quotients of connective exact dg categories by a class of projective-injective objects.
We show that each connective exact dg category can be obtained in this manner from the dg quotient of a Quillen exact category.
We end this section with several classes of examples including stable dg categories, Cohen–Macaulay dg modules and Higgs categories.
Relative cluster categories are then described as the bounded derived categories of the corresponding Higgs dg categories.

In Section
4
, we show that “functor dg categories” with exact target naturally inherit exact structures from the target.
Building upon this, we show that a specific monoidal structure on the category of small connective exact dg categories is closed.
In Section
5
, we apply our theoretic framework to study the class of
0
-Auslander extriangulated categories.
Within this context, we resolve the algebraic case of a conjecture posed by Fang–Gorsky–Palu–Plamondon–Pressland.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we collect basic results on extriangulated categories,
and some basic notations and terminology needed in this paper.
For more details on extriangulated category,
we refer to
[
31
,
34
,
15
,
16
]
.

2.1.
\delta
-functors in extriangulated categories

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be a skeletally small extriangulated category.

Lemma 2.1

(
[
30
]
, Proposition 1.20)
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

be any
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle, let
f:A\rightarrow D
any morphism, and let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\delta}

be any
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle realizing
f_{*}\delta
.
Then there is a morphism
g
which gives a morphism of
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\delta}

and moreover, the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\oplus B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}{-}f\\
x\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}d{,}\;g{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{*}\delta}

becomes an
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle.

The following diagram lemma presents a slightly stronger version of
[
31
, Proposition 3.15]
.
It is often used as a replacement for the non-existent push-outs or pull-backs in extriangulated categories
[
34
]
.

Lemma 2.2
.

Suppose we are given
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
,
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{\prime}}

Then there is a commutative diagram in
\mathcal{C}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{1}}

which satisfies

\mathfrak{s}(y_{2}^{*}\delta)=[A_{1}\xrightarrow{m_{1}}M\xrightarrow{e_{1}}B_{2}],

\mathfrak{s}(y_{1}^{*}\delta^{\prime})=[A_{2}\xrightarrow{m_{2}}M\xrightarrow{e_{2}}B_{1}],

(m_{1})_{*}(\delta)+(m_{2})_{*}(\delta^{\prime})=0

and moreover, the sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\oplus B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}{e_{1}}\\
e_{2}\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}y_{1}{,}-y_{2}{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{1})_{*}(\delta)}

is an
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle.

Proof.

We apply the dual of Lemma
2.1
to the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}^{*}(\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

Then there exists a morphism
e_{2}:M\rightarrow B_{1}
such that the above diagram commutes in
\mathcal{C}
and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\oplus B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}{e_{2}}\\
e_{1}\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}y_{1}{,}-y_{2}{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m_{1})_{*}(\delta)}

is an
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle.

We then apply
[
31
, Proposition 3.17]
to the following diagram

So we have an
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

such that the above diagram commutes (so we have
k=e_{2}
) and

(i)

(m_{2})_{*}(-\delta^{\prime})=(m_{1})_{*}(\delta)
,

(ii)

(x_{2})_{*}(\theta)=0
,

(iii)

[1,0]^{*}(\theta)+[y_{1},-y_{2}]^{*}(-\delta^{\prime})=0
.

Applying the functor
\begin{bmatrix}1\\
0\end{bmatrix}^{*}
to the last equality, we obtain
\theta=y_{1}^{*}(\delta^{\prime})
.
∎

Definition 2.3
.

A contravariant connected sequence of functors is a pair
(T,\epsilon)
where
T=(T^{i})_{i\geq 0}
is sequence of right
\mathcal{C}
-modules and
\epsilon
is a collection of morphisms
\epsilon_{\delta}^{i}:T^{i}(A)\rightarrow T^{i+1}(C)
for each
\mathbb{E}\mbox{-}
extension
\delta\in\mathbb{E}(C,A)
and
i\geq 0
which is natural with respect to morphisms of
\mathbb{E}
-extensions.
It is a right
\delta
-functor if for any
\mathfrak{s}\mbox{-}
triangle

the associated sequence (which is a complex by the naturality of
\epsilon
, cf.
[
15
, Proposition 3.20]
)

\ldots\rightarrow T^{n}(C)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(p)}T^{n}(B)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(k)}T^{n}(A)\xrightarrow{\epsilon^{n}_{\delta}}T^{n+1}(C)\rightarrow\ldots

is exact.
A morphism
(T,\epsilon)\rightarrow(\widetilde{T},\widetilde{\epsilon})
of right
\delta
-functors is a family of morphisms of right
\mathcal{C}
-modules
\theta^{i}:T^{i}\rightarrow\widetilde{T}^{i}
which is compatible with the connecting morphisms, i.e., for each
\delta\in\mathbb{E}(C,A)
and each

Dually one defines the notion of covariant connected sequence of functors and left
\delta
-functors.

We have the following extriangulated analogue of
[
17
, Proposition 2.1]
.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
(T,\epsilon)
and
(R,\eta)
be right
\delta
-functors such that
R^{i}
is weakly effaceable for each
i>0
.
Then each morphism
R^{0}\rightarrow T^{0}
extends uniquely to a morphism of right
\delta
-functors
(R,\eta)\rightarrow(T,\epsilon)
.

Proof.

Suppose we have constructed natural morphisms
\theta^{j}:R^{j}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}T^{j}
which are compatible with the connecting morphisms for
0\leq j<i
.

Let
C
be an object in
\mathcal{C}
and
x\in R^{i}(C)
an arbitrary element.
Since
R^{i}
is effaceable, there exists a
\mathbb{E}
-triangle

By the exactness of the top horizontal sequence, there exists an element
y\in R^{i-1}(A)
such that
\eta_{\delta}^{i-1}(y)=x
.
Since the left square commutes by the construction of
\theta^{i-1}
, it is clear that the element
\epsilon_{\delta}^{i-1}\circ\theta_{A}^{i-1}(y)
is independent of the choice of
y
.
Let

This implies that
\epsilon_{\delta}^{i-1}\circ\theta_{A}^{i-1}(y)
is independent of the choice of the
\mathbb{E}
-triangle.
Similarly as above, one shows that the map
\theta^{i}_{C}:R^{i}(C)\rightarrow T^{i}(C)
is a morphism of abelian groups.
It is direct to verify that
\theta^{i}:R^{i}\rightarrow T^{i}
is a natural transformation
and it follows directly from the construction that
\theta^{i}
is compatible with the connecting morphisms.
By induction on
i
, we have natural transformations
\theta^{i}:R^{i}\rightarrow T^{i}
for
i\geq 0
which are compatible with the connecting morphisms. The uniqueness is also clear from the construction of the
\theta^{i}
.
∎

.

\mathbb{E}

the associated complexes

\ldots\rightarrow T^{n}(C,-)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(p,-)}T^{n}(B,-)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(k,-)}T^{n}(A,-)\xrightarrow{\epsilon^{n}_{\delta}}T^{n+1}(C,-)\rightarrow\ldots

\ldots\rightarrow T^{n}(?,A)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(?,k)}T^{n}(?,B)\xrightarrow{T^{n}(?,p)}T^{n}(?,C)\xrightarrow{\eta^{n}_{\delta}}T^{n+1}(?,A)\rightarrow\ldots

are exact in
\mathrm{Mod}\,\mathcal{C}
, resp., in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{Mod}\,
.
A morphism
(T,\epsilon,\eta)\rightarrow(\widetilde{T},\widetilde{\epsilon},\widetilde{\eta})
of
\delta
-functors is a family of morphisms of
\mathcal{C}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodules
\theta^{i}:T^{i}\rightarrow\widetilde{T}^{i}
which is compatible with the connecting morphisms.

Let
(T,\epsilon,\eta)
a
\delta
-functor.
We assume that

(2.5.1)

T^{0}=\mathrm{Hom}(?,-)
and
T^{i}
are effaceable for
i>0
.

By Proposition
2.4
, we have that
T^{i}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{E}^{i}
for
i>0
which is compatible with the connecting morphisms
\epsilon
.
We see that
\epsilon_{\delta}^{0}:\mathrm{Hom}(A,-)\rightarrow\mathbb{E}(C,-)
is given by

(\epsilon_{\delta}^{0})_{U}:\mathrm{Hom}(A,U)\rightarrow\mathbb{E}(C,U),\;\;f\mapsto f_{*}\delta

for each object
U\in\mathcal{C}
.

(2.5.2)

We assume that a similar description holds for
\eta_{\delta}^{0}
.

and any two

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.36572pt}{-27.12347pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -6.04 -37.53)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\;.

Put
\mathbb{E}^{0}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(?,-)
.
From the definition of
\mathbb{E}^{n}
and the construction of the connecting morphisms, cf.
[
15
, Claim 3.11]
, it is clear that
(\mathbb{E}^{n})_{n\geq 0}
together with its connecting morphisms satisfy the above assumptions (
2.5.1
–
2.5.3
).

Corollary 2.6
.

Keep the notations as above.
Let
(R,\gamma,\mu)
be another
\delta
-functor which satisfies the above assumptions (
2.5.1
–
2.5.3
).
Then there is a unique isomorphism of
\delta
-functors
\theta^{i}:R^{i}\rightarrow T^{i}

for
i\geq 0
such that
\theta^{0}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(?,-)}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.4
, we have that for each
i\geq 0
, the right
\mathcal{C}
-module
R^{i}(?,A)
is naturally isomorphic to
T^{i}(?,A)
in
\mathrm{Mod}\,\mathcal{C}
and the isomorphism is natural in the variable
A
and compatible with the connecting morphisms.
Hence we have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{C}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodules
\theta^{i}:R^{i}\rightarrow T^{i}
for each
i\geq 0
.
Similarly
R^{i}(C,-)
is naturally isomorphic to
T^{i}(C,-)
and this also gives an isomorphism of
\mathcal{C}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodules
\lambda^{i}:R^{i}\rightarrow T^{i}
for each
i\geq 0
.

We identify both
R^{1}
and
T^{1}
with
\mathbb{E}
and by assumption the natural isomorphisms
\theta^{1}
and
\lambda^{1}
are both given by the identity.
We only need to check that the natural isomorphisms
\theta^{i}
and
\lambda^{i}
coincide with each other for each
i\geq 2
and we proceed by induction on
i
.

Let
C
and
F
be objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
x\in R^{i}(C,F)
for
i\geq 1
.
Since
R^{i}
and
R^{i-1}
are both effaceable, there exists
\mathfrak{s}
-triangles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

such that there exists an element
y\in R^{i-2}(A,H)
with
x=\mu_{\psi}^{i-1}\gamma_{\delta}^{i-2}(y)=\gamma_{\delta}^{i-1}\mu_{\psi}^{i-2}(y)
where the second equality is given by the commutativity of the diagram
2.5.4
for the bimodules
R^{i}
,
R^{i-1}
and
R^{i-2}
.
Since by induction
\theta^{j}
coincides with
\lambda^{j}
for
j=i-2
and
i-1
, we obtain that the isomorphisms
\theta^{i}
and
\lambda^{i}
coincide with each other and this finishes the proof.
∎

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be a skeletally small extriangulated category.
The underlying category
\mathcal{C}
being additive, one has the
split Grothendieck group

K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(\mathcal{C})
defined as the quotient of the free abelian group generated by the isomorphism classes
[X]
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
by the subgroup generated by the elements
[X]-[Y]+[Z]
for each split short exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

in
\mathcal{C}
. The image of
[X]
in
K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(\mathcal{C})
is denoted by
[X]^{\mathrm{sp}}
.

Definition 2.7

(
[
41
]
)
.

The Grothendieck group
K_{0}(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
of the extriangulated category
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is the abelian group

K_{0}(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})\coloneqq K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(\mathcal{C})/\langle[X]^{\mathrm{sp}}-[Y]^{\mathrm{sp}}+[Z]^{\mathrm{sp}}\mid\text{(\ref{conf:C}) is an $\mathfrak{s}$-triangle}\rangle

(2.7.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z.}

2.2.
Recollections on dg categories

In this subsection, we collect basic notations and terminology needed in this paper.
The standard references for dg categories are
[
24
,
25
,
12
,
38
,
3
]
.

Throughout we fix a commutative ring
k
.
We write
\otimes
for the tensor product over
k
.
We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(k)
the dg category of complexes of
k
-modules and by
\mathcal{C}(k)
the category of complexes of
k
-modules.
Let
M
be complex of
k
-modules. We put

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}M=(\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{0}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots).}

For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, denote by
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
the dg category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}

and whose morphism complexes are given by

(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})).

The composition is naturally induced by that of
\mathcal{A}
.
A dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
connective
if for each pair of objects
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathcal{A}
, the
complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
has cohomology concentrated in non-positive degrees.
It is called
strictly connective
if the components of the complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
vanish
in all positive degrees.
Following Toën, the dg category
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
is called the
connective cover
of
\mathcal{A}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg
k
-category.
For two objects
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, the Hom complex is denoted by
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
or

\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})
. An element
f
of
\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{p}
will be called a
homogeneous
morphism of degree
p
with the notation
|f|=p
.
A homogeneous morphism
f:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{2}
is
closed
if we have
d(f)=0
.

We denote by
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
the category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and whose morphism
spaces are defined by

(Z^{0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=Z^{0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})),

where
Z^{0}
is the kernel of
d:\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{1}
.
Similarly, we denote by
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
the category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and whose
morphism spaces are given by

(H^{0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=H^{0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})),

where
H^{0}
denotes the zeroth cohomology of the complex.
A dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
additive
if
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is additive as a category.

The
oppositie dg category

\mathcal{A}^{op}
has the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and its morphism spaces are defined by

\mathcal{A}^{op}(X,Y)=\mathcal{A}(Y,X);

the composition of
f\in\mathcal{A}^{op}(Y,X)^{p}
with
g\in\mathcal{A}^{op}(Z,Y)^{q}
is given by
(-1)^{pq}gf
.
By a dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
, we mean a right dg
\mathcal{A}
-module, i.e., a dg functor
\mathcal{A}^{op}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(k)
.
We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
the dg category of right dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules. The category of dg

\mathcal{A}
-modules is

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})=Z^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})).

The category up to homotopy of dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules is

\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{A})=H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})).

For each object
A\in\mathcal{A}
, we have the right dg module
represented by

A

A^{\wedge}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(-,A).

A dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
is
representable
if it is isomorphic to
A^{\wedge}
for some

A\in\mathcal{A}
and
quasi-representable
if it is quasi-isomorphic to
A^{\wedge}
for some
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
The
Yoneda dg functor

\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}),\;\;f:A\rightarrow A^{\prime}\mapsto f^{\wedge}:A^{\wedge}\rightarrow A^{\prime\wedge}

is fully faithful.

A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a
quasi-equivalence
if

a)

it is
quasi-fully faithful
, i.e., for all objects
A,A^{\prime}\in A
, the morphism

F_{A,A^{\prime}}:\mathcal{A}(A,A^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(FA,FA^{\prime}).

is a quasi-isomorphism, and

b)

the induced functor
H^{0}(F):H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
is an equivalence of categories.

The category
\mathrm{dgcat}
of small dg categories admits the Dwyer-Kan model structure (cf.
[
36
]
),
whose weak equivalences are the quasi-equivalences. Its homotopy category is denoted by
\mathrm{Hqe}
.
There exists a cofibrant replacement functor
Q
on
\mathrm{dgcat}
such that for any
\mathcal{A}\in\mathrm{dgcat}
, the natural dg functor
Q(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
is the identity on the set of objects.
In particular, the Hom-complexes of
Q(\mathcal{A})
are cofibrant over
k
.
The
tensor product

\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}
of two dg categories
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
has the class of objects
\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{A})\times\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{B})
and the morphism spaces

(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B})((A,B),(A^{\prime},B^{\prime}))=\mathcal{A}(A,A^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{B}(B,B^{\prime})

with the natural compositions and units.
This defines a symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes-
on
\mathrm{dgcat}
which is closed.
For
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{dgcat}
, put
\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}\otimes Q(\mathcal{B})
.
This extends to a bifunctor
-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-:\mathrm{dgcat}\times\mathrm{dgcat}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}
and then passes through the homotopy categories

-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-:\mathrm{Hqe}\times\mathrm{Hqe}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hqe}.

For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
its
derived category
, a triangulated category.
By definition,
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is the localization of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
at the class of
quasi-isomorphisms
, i.e., morphisms of dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules which induce quasi-isomorphisms of complexes when evaluated at any object in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\pi:\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
be the quotient functor.
For a morphism
j:M\rightarrow N
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
, we denote by
\overline{\jmath}=\pi(j):M\rightarrow N
the corresponding morphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
.
A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
induces a triangle functor
F_{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
which is an equivalence of triangulated categories if
F
is a quasi-equivalence.
The
dg derived category

\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
of
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of cofibrant dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules in the
projective model structure
of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
(cf.
[
25
, Theorem 3.2]
).
The canonical functor
H^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.
For dg categories
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, we define
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
to be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
whose objects are the dg bimodules
X
such that
X(-,B)
is quasi-representable for each object
B
of
\mathcal{B}
.
We define the canonical dg enhancement
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
to be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
whose objects are those of
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
A dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
pretriangulated
if the canonical inclusion
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is a triangulated subcategory.
Note that
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is pretriangulated if
\mathcal{A}
is pretriangulated.
For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we have its
pretriangulated hull

\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
(
[
3
,
12
,
4
]
).
We denote the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A}))
by
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
.
The Yoneda dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
extends to a fully faithful dg functor
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
.
It induces a fully faithful triangle functor
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
whose essential image is the triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
generated by the representable dg modules.

For a small category
\mathcal{I}
, we have the
k
-category
k\mathcal{I}
whose set of objects is the same as that of
\mathcal{I}
and for each pair of objects
x,y
in
\mathcal{C}
, the space
k\mathcal{I}(i,j)
is the free
k
-module generated by the set
\mathcal{I}(i,j)
.
The
square
category
\mathrm{Sq}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 00}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

with the commutativity relation
jf\sim kg
;
the
cospan
category
\mathrm{Cosp}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11;}

the
span
category
\mathrm{Sp}
is the path category of the quiver

Lemma 2.8

(
[
26
,
2
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg category.
There is a canonical t-structure

(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0})

on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
such that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0}
is formed by those dg modules whose cohomology is concentrated in non-negative degrees.

Note that we have
A^{\wedge}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
for each
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
When
\mathcal{A}
is connective, then
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
is formed by those dg modules whose cohomology is concentrated in non-positive degrees.
If
\mathcal{A}
is strictly connective, then for each dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
, the truncation
\tau_{\leq 0}M
becomes a dg
\mathcal{A}
-submodule and the canonical triangle for
M
is induced by the short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}M\rightarrow M\rightarrow\tau_{\geq 1}M\rightarrow 0
of dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

Proposition 2.9
.

(
[
5
, Proposition 6.2.1]
)

Suppose
\mathcal{A}
is a connective dg category.
Put
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
.
Let
\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0}
(resp.,
\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
) be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
which consists of objects isomorphic to those of the form
A=(\oplus_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}[r_{i}],q)
for
n\geq 0
and
r_{i}\geq 0
(resp.,
r_{i}\leq 0
) for each
i
.
Then
(\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0},\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0})
is a co-t-structure on
\mathcal{T}
, the canonical co-t-structure.

2.3.
Exact dg categories

In this subsection, we briefly recall the definitions of homotopy short exact sequences and exact dg categories.
For more details, we refer to
[
9
]
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be the dg
k
-path category of the following quiver with relations:

(2.9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

where
f
and
g
are closed of degree 0, and
gf=0
.

Definition 2.10

(
[
9
, Definition 3.14]
)
.

The category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of 3-term homotopy complexes (=3-term h-complexes) over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the 0-th homology category
H^{0}(\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A}))
of the dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A})
of strictly unital
A_{\infty}
-functors
from
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathcal{A}
, cf.
[
29
]
.

We unpack the definition that we will actually work with as follows.
We identify objects
X
in
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
with diagrams in
\mathcal{A}

(2.10.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
|f|=|j|=0
,
|h|=-1
and
d(f)=0
,
d(j)=0
and
d(h)=-jf
.
Suppose
X^{\prime}
is given by the diagram where a superscript
′
is added to each object and each morphism.
A morphism from
X
to
X^{\prime}
is given by an equivalence class of 6-tuples
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
,
i=0
,
1
,
2
,
j=1
,
2
, where

r_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}^{\prime},|r_{i}|=0,d(r_{i})=0,

s_{j}:A_{j-1}\rightarrow A_{j}^{\prime},|s_{j}|=-1,d(s_{1})=f^{\prime}r_{0}-r_{1}f,

d(s_{2})=j^{\prime}r_{1}-r_{2}j,

and

t:A_{0}\rightarrow A_{2}^{\prime},|t|=-2,d(t)=r_{2}\circ h-h^{\prime}\circ r_{0}-s_{2}\circ f-j^{\prime}\circ s_{1},

which can be identified with the following diagram:

A 3-term homotopy complex over
\mathcal{A}
(
2.10.1
) yields a canonical morphism in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

u:A_{0}^{\wedge}\rightarrow\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j^{\wedge}:A_{1}^{\wedge}\rightarrow A_{2}^{\wedge}),

where
u=\begin{bmatrix}f^{\wedge}\\
h^{\wedge}\end{bmatrix}
.

Definition 2.11

(
[
9
, Lemma 3.22]
)
.

A
3
-term h-complex (
2.10.1
) is homotopy left exact if and only if the map
\tau_{\leq 0}(u)
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
-modules. Dually, we define the notion of homotopy right exact sequence and then the notion of homotopy short exact sequence.

A
homotopy square

[
9
, Definition 3.21]
in
\mathcal{A}
is a diagram in
\mathcal{A}

(2.11.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

where
i
,
j
,
p
and
q
are closed morphisms of degree
0
, and
h
is a morphism of degree
-1
such that
d(h)=pi-qj
, yields a canonical
3
-term h-complex

(2.11.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}i\\
j\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}p{,}-q{]}}

The homotopy square (
2.11.1
) is a
homotopy pullback square
(=
homotopy cartesian square
) if the
3
-term h-complex (
2.11.2
) is homotopy left exact.
Dually, we have the notions of
homotopy pushout square
(=
homotopy cocartesian square
) and
homotopy bicartesian square
.

Theorem 2.12

(
[
6
]
cf. also
[
14
]
)
.

We have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(-,-)\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(-,-)

of bifunctors
\mathrm{Hqe}_{k-\mathrm{cf}}^{op}\times\mathrm{Hqe}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hqe}
, where
\mathrm{Hqe}_{k-\mathrm{cf}}
is the
full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}
whose objects are the dg categories whose morphism
complexes are cofibrant dg
k
-modules.

Corollary 2.13
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be a full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be a small dg category.
Let
F:\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
be the functor induced by the inclusion
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
.
Then an object
X\in\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
lies in the essential image of
F
if and only if for each object
B
in
\mathcal{B}
,
X(-,B)
is quasi-isomorphic to a dg
\mathcal{A}
-module represented by an object in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
be the closure of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
under homotopy equivalences in
\mathcal{A}
.
The inclusion of

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
into
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
is a quasi-equivalence and therefore induces a quasi-equivalence in

\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},?)
.
By the above theorem, it also induces a quasi-equivalence in

\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},?)
.
Thus, we may assume that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is stable under homotopy equivalences
in
\mathcal{A}
.
Now the inclusion
\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Fun}_{A_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is clearly an isomorphism
onto the full subcategory of
A_{\infty}
-functors whose values on objects lie in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
Thanks to the theorem, this implies the statement.
∎

Definition 2.14

(
[
9
, Definition 4.1]
)
.

An exact structure on
\mathcal{A}
is a class
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
stable under isomorphisms, consisting of homotopy short exact sequences (called conflations)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
i
is called an inflation and
p
is called a deflation, such that the following axioms are satisfied

Ex0

\mathrm{id}_{0}
is a deflation.

Ex1

Compositions of deflations are deflations.

Ex2

Given a deflation
p:B\rightarrow C
and any map
c:C^{\prime}\rightarrow C
in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, the object

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

admits a homotopy pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

and
{p^{\prime}}
is also a deflation.

\mathrm{Ex}2^{op}

Given an inflation
i:A\rightarrow B
and any map
a:A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, the object

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}

admits a homotopy pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}

and
{i^{\prime}}
is also an inflation.

We call
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
or simply
\mathcal{A}
an exact dg category.

Definition 2.15

(
[
9
, Definition 4.3]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
be exact dg categories.
A morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
is exact if the induced functor
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
sends objects in
\mathcal{S}
to objects in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.
We denote by
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}((\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}),(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}))
the subset of
\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
consisiting of exact morphisms.
We denote by
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}
the subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}
consisting of exact dg categories with exact morphisms.
An exact morphism
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is an exact quasi-equivalence if it is an isomorphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}
.

Examples 2.16
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive dg category.

1)

If
\mathcal{A}
is pretriangulated, then the class of all homotopy short exact sequences determines an exact structure on
\mathcal{A}
, cf.
[
9
, Example 4.7]
.

2)

If
\mathcal{A}
is concentrated in degree zero, the exact dg structures on
\mathcal{A}
are the same as the Quillen exact structures on
\mathcal{A}
, cf.
[
9
, Example 4.6]
.

3)

The notion of exact dg structure is self-dual, cf.
[
9
, Proposition 4.9 d)]
.

4)

Let
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be a quasi-equivalence of dg categories.
Then the quasi-equivalence
F
induces a bijection
between the class of exact dg structures on
\mathcal{A}
and that on
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
Moreover, for each exact structure on
\mathcal{A}
and the exact structure on
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
induced by this bijection, the dg functor
F
induces an equivalence of extriangulated categories between
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
and
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}),\mathbb{E}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
, cf.
[
9
, Remark 4.5 b)]
.

5)

Any additive dg category
\mathcal{A}
with the closure under isomorphisms of the class of all split exact sequences is an exact dg category.
We call this the trivial exact structure.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be another exact dg category.
Then any dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is exact when
\mathcal{A}
is endowed with the trivial exact structure.

6)

A full dg subcategory
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
of an exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
is extension-closed provided that for each conflation in
\mathcal{A}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

if
A
and
C
belong to
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, then so does
B
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is an exact dg category and
\mathcal{B}
is an extension-closed subcategory, then
\mathcal{B}
inherits an exact structure, cf.
[
8
, Example-Definition 5.7]
.

3.
Embedding theorem for connective exact dg categories

Throughout this section, let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be an exact dg category.
It is
connective
if the underlying dg category
\mathcal{A}
is connective, i.e., the Hom complexes
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)
has cohomology concentrated in non-positive degrees for all
X
,
Y
in
\mathcal{A}
.
By Example
2.16
4), we may assume that
\mathcal{A}
has cofibrant Hom complexes.
One of the main aims of this section is to show the following theorem.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be an exact dg category.
There exists a universal exact morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
from
\mathcal{A}
to a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is connective, this morphism satisfies:

1)

It induces a quasi-equivalence from
\tau_{\leq 0}{\mathcal{A}}
to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
for an extension-closed dg subcategory
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
;

2)

It induces a natural bijection
\mathbb{E}(C,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(FC,FA)
for each pair of objects
C,A
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
where
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})=H^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
.

We call
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
the bounded dg derived category of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

We will prove Theorem
3.1
after Lemma
3.13
.
To avoid the heavy use of the symbol
\wedge
, we will omit it for representable dg modules.

Example 3.2
.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a Quillen exact category, then
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is quasi-equivalent to the canonical dg enhancement of the bounded derived category of
\mathcal{A}
, whence the name.

Remark 3.3
.

The analogous theorem for exact
\infty
-categories
[
1
]
in the sense of Barwick is
due to Klemenc
[
28
]
.

3.1.
Construction of the universal morphism

Let
\mathcal{N}
be the full triangulated subcategory of
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
generated by the total dg modules
N
of conflations

So
N
is defined by the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})

(
\bigstar
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}-f\\
-h\end{bmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}}
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}\;0{]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-h{,}j{]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-1\ 0{]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\\
1\end{bmatrix}}

where we omit the symbol
\wedge
for representable dg modules and where

s=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}\>,\;U=\mathrm{Cone}(f)\,\>,\;V=\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j)\mbox{ and }N=\mathrm{Cone}([-h,\;j]).

Let
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the full dg subcategory of
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of the objects in
\mathcal{N}
.
Let
F
be the canonical morphism from
\mathcal{A}
to the Drinfeld dg quotient
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dg}}

(recall that we assume the morphism complexes of
\mathcal{A}
to be cofibrant hence flat). We consider

F
as a morphism in the category
\mathrm{Hqe}
obtained from the category of small dg categories
by localizing at the class of quasi-equivalences.

Lemma 3.4
.

The morphism

F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dg}}
is the universal exact morphism (cf. Definition
2.15
) from
\mathcal{A}
to a pretriangulated dg category.

Proof.

Put
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})=H^{0}(\mathrm{pretr}(A)/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dg}})
.
We first show that
F
is exact.
Let
X
be a conflation as follows

We consider the following roof
[1,0]/[-h,j]
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-h{,}j{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}0{]}}

where
U
is the cone of
f:A\rightarrow B
.
We have the following triangle in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \Sigma A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f/1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j/1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1}{,}0{]}{/}{[}-h{,}j{]}}

So the morphism
F
is exact.

We show that
F
is universal.
Let
G:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
be an exact morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}
from
\mathcal{A}
to a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{B}
.
By the universal property of
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
, there exists a unique morphism from
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
to
\mathcal{B}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
which extends
G
.
Since
G
is an exact morphism,
H^{0}(G)
sends the totalization
N
of
X
to a zero object in
H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
.
By the universal property of Drinfeld dg quotient
[
37
]
, there exists a unique morphism in

\mathrm{Hqe}
from
\mathrm{pretr}(A)/\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{dg}}
to
\mathcal{B}
.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

For a connective exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
, the universal exact morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
induces an isomorphism
K_{0}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow K_{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
, where
K_{0}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))
denotes the Grothendieck group of the extriangulated category
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.

Proof.

We assume that
\mathcal{A}
is strictly connective.
The dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
induces a dg functor between the pretriangulated hulls
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{pretr}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))
.
We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pretr}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tr}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))=\mathcal{H}^{b}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))}

By
[
7
, Corollary B]
, we have
K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A})))
.
Hence the canonical map
K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
is injective.
Since
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
is generated as a triangulated category by the objects in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, it is also surjective.
Hence we have
K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
.

By definition, we have
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{N}
where
\mathcal{N}
is the full triangulated subcategory of
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
generated by the totalizations of the conflations.
By
[
18
, VIII 3.1]
, we have that
K_{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
is the quotient of
K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
by the subgroup generated by
[N]\in K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
where
N
runs through the totalizations of conflations in
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
Note that in (

★

‣
3.1
) we have
[N]=[C]-[U]=[C]-[B]+[A]
in
K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
.
It follows that the isomorphism
K_{0}^{\mathrm{sp}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}K_{0}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A}))
induces a canonical isomorphism

K_{0}(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}K_{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})).

∎

Denote by
\mathcal{H}
the heart of the canonical t-structure
(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0})
, cf. Lemma
2.8
.
We denote by
H^{0}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
the associated homological functor.
Recall that we omit the symbol
\wedge
for representable dg modules.

Lemma 3.6
.

A 3-term h-complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

is exact if and only if its totalization
N
lies in
\mathcal{H}
. In this case, the totalization
N
is the cokernel of
H^{0}(B)\xrightarrow{H^{0}(j)}H^{0}(C)
in
\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

Consider the diagram (

★

‣
3.1
).
For
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
and
i\leq 0
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(A^{\prime},\Sigma^{i}A)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(A^{\prime},\Sigma^{i}V)

and so we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(A^{\prime},\Sigma^{i-1}N)=0
and hence
N\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0}
.
Since representable dg modules lie in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
,
it is clear from the construction that
N\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
.
Therefore, we have
N\in\mathcal{H}
.

From the rightmost column of the diagram (

★

‣
3.1
), we have
H^{1}(V)\xrightarrow{\sim}N
.
From the second row of (

★

‣
3.1
), we have an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(B)=0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(j)}

So the second statement follows.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

We have an exact sequence in
\mathcal{H}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(j)}

where, with the notations of (

★

‣
3.1
), the image of the morphism
H^{0}(j)
is
H^{0}(U)
.
Using the canonical extriangulated category
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
, this is equivalent to saying that
N
is the defect (cf.
[
33
, Definition 2.4]
) of the conflation
A\xrightarrow{\overline{f}}B\xrightarrow{\overline{\jmath}}C

in
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.

Notation 3.8
.

We denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
the essential image of
\mathcal{N}
under the homological functor
H^{0}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
.

Definition 3.9
.

An object in
\mathcal{H}
is defective, if it is the cokernel of a map

H^{0}(B)\xrightarrow{H^{0}(\overline{j})}H^{0}(C)\>,\;

where
j
is a deflation, or equivalently if it is the totalization of some conflation.

Let
X_{i}
,
i=1,2
, be homotopy short exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{i}}

and
\alpha:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}
a morphism of
{\mathcal{H}}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
. Let

X_{3}
be homotopy square in
\mathcal{A}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}

obtained by restricting a representative of
\alpha
.
Notice that by
[
9
, Lemma 3.22]
, up to (non canonical) isomorphism,
the object
X_{3}
is independent of the choice of representative.
Suppose that
\alpha
restricts to an isomorphism
C_{1}\rightarrow C_{2}
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Then
X_{3}
is homotopy cartesian by
[
9
, Proposition 3.31]
.
Let
N_{i}
be the totalization of
X_{i}
for
i=1
,
2
,
3
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Keep the notations as above. The following statements hold.

a)

There exists a triangle in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

N_{3}\rightarrow N_{1}\rightarrow N_{2}\rightarrow\Sigma N_{3}

b)

If
X_{1}
is a conflation, the triangle in a) induces a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{H}

0\rightarrow N_{3}\rightarrow N_{1}\xrightarrow{g}N_{2}\rightarrow 0

where
g
is the morphism induced by
\alpha
.

Notice that if
X_{1}
is a conflation, then
X_{2}
is a conflation by the dual of
[
9
, Proposition 4.9 b)]

and hence
X_{3}
is homotopy bicartesian by the dual of
[
9
, Lemma 4.12]
.

Proof.

a)
Suppose the morphism
\alpha:X_{1}\rightarrow X_{2}
is given by the following diagram in
\mathcal{A}
:

Since
\overline{c}
is an isomorphism in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, we may replace
C_{1}
by
C_{2}
,
j_{1}:B_{1}\rightarrow C_{1}
by
c\circ j_{1}
and
h_{1}
by
c\circ h_{1}
.
Thus we may assume
C_{1}=C_{2}
and
c=\mathrm{id}_{C_{1}}
in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Then we have the following diagram in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\oplus A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}b{,}-f_{2}{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}0{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}

where we have omitted a zero diagonal map and the homotopy
h_{1}:A_{1}\rightarrow C_{1}
and where
\varphi=\begin{bmatrix}f_{1}\\
a\end{bmatrix}
,
\psi={[}-s_{2}{,}-h_{2}{]}
.
It induces a canonical diagram in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cone}(\varphi)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cone}(f_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\\
1\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}-f_{2}\\
-h_{2}\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}1\ 0\ 0\\
0\ 1\ 0\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-h_{1}{,}j_{1}{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}0{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}

where
u={[}-s_{1}{,}b{,}-f_{2}{]}
,
s^{\prime}=[t,s_{2},h_{2}]
and where the first row is a graded-split short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
.
By the
3\times 3
-lemma, we have a triangle in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

N_{3}\rightarrow N_{1}\rightarrow N_{2}\rightarrow\Sigma N_{3}

which gives the short exact sequence of b) in
\mathcal{H}
when
X_{1}
is a conflation.
∎

3.2.
The canonical t-structure on the category
\mathcal{N}

In the rest of this section, we keep the assumption that
\mathcal{A}
is connective. In this case, we have

•

The left aisle
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
is formed by those dg modules whose cohomology is concentrated in non-positive degrees.

•

The heart
\mathcal{H}
of the t-structure
(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0})
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Mod}\,H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
and the cohomological functor
H^{0}
sends a dg module
M
to
H^{0}(M):A\mapsto H^{0}(M(A))
.

•

In particular
H^{0}
is fully faithful on the full subcategory of quasi-representable dg modules and
H^{0}(M)
is projective in the heart for each quasi-representable dg module
M
.

Recall that a full subcategory of an abelian category is
wide
if it is stable under extensions,
kernels and cokernels. In particular, each wide subcategory is abelian and its inclusion is fully
faithful and fully exact.

Lemma 3.11
.

The full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}
, consisting of defective objects (cf. Definition
3.9
), is a wide subcategoy.

Corollary 3.12
.

An object in
\mathcal{H}
lies in
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
(cf. Notation
3.8
) if and only if it is defective.
The t-structure
(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0})
on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
restricts to a bounded t-structure on
\mathcal{N}
.

Proof of the Corollary.

Let
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{N}
consisting of objects
N
such that
H^{i}(N)
is defective for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and only finitely many of them are nonzero.
By Lemma
3.11
, this is a triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{N}
. It certainly contains totalizations of conflations and thus is equal to
\mathcal{N}
.
Therefore the objects
N
in
\mathcal{N}
have the property that
H^{i}(N)
is defective for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and only finitely many of them is nonzero.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.11
.

Denote by
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{H}
consisting of defective objects. Consider a
three-term complex

(3.12.1)

\displaystyle P\rightarrow Q\rightarrow R

in
\mathrm{Mod}\,H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
which is exact in the middle.

(1) We first show that
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
is closed under extensions.
Assume the sequence (
3.12.1
) is a short exact sequence with
P
and
R
defective.
Suppose
P
and
R
are totalizations of
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
respectively, where
X_{i}
,
i=1,2
, is of the form

Then
P
is the cokernel of

and

is the cokernel of

.
By the horseshoe lemma,

is the cokernel of

H^{0}(B_{1}\oplus B_{2})\xrightarrow{\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\jmath}_{1}\ \ \overline{h}\\

in

, where

is a morphism in

.
Since the morphism

0\ \ {j}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}C_{1}\oplus C_{2}

can be written as the following composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\oplus B_{2}}

it is a deflation by the axioms

and

. Therefore

(2) We show that

is closed under kernels.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}B_{1}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}C_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\overline{\jmath_{1}})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\overline{\jmath_{2}})}

.

\mu:j_{1}\rightarrow j_{2}
in

which induces a morphism

is isomorphic to

and thus is defective.

(3) We show that
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
is closed under cokernels.

,

.

is the totalization of a conflation.
This shows that

is also defective.
∎

The following lemma is standard.

Lemma 3.13

(
[
19
]
, Lemma 3.3)
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a triangulated category with a thick subcategory
\mathcal{S}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
admits a torsion pair (
[
20
, Definition 2.2]
)
\mathcal{S}=(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
.
Put
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{X}\cap\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{Y}
.
Then for any
A\in{{}^{\perp}\mathcal{Y}}
and
B\in\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{X}^{\perp}
, where the symbol
⟂
stands for the Hom orthogonal, the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}/[\mathcal{P}]}(A,B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{S}}(A,B)

is a bijection.

In the above lemma, we have
\mathcal{P}=0
when
\mathcal{S}=(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
is in particular a t-structure.

3.1
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C.}

So the object

is isomorphic to

and the image of

under the functor

is an extension-closed subcategory of
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

It remains to show that
H^{0}(F)
induces isomorphisms

for objects

and

in

.

, then
