and
X^{\prime}
give equivalent roofs.
So we have a well-defined map

\mathbb{E}(C,A)\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(C,A)
.
From the above proof, this map is a surjection.
From the definition of the bifunctor
\mathbb{E}
, it is clear that this map is natural in the variables
C
and
A
.
From the definition of the addition operation on
\mathbb{E}(C,A)
, it is clear that this map is additive.
Now suppose that
[X]
is an element in
\mathbb{E}(C,A)
which is sent to zero in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(C,A)
.
Suppose that
X
is of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

Then the morphism
\overline{f}/1:A\rightarrow B
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is a split monomorphism.
Since
H^{0}(F)
is fully faithful, the morphism
\overline{f}:A\rightarrow B
is a split monomorphism in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
By
2)\Rightarrow 1)
in
[
9
, Proposition 4.19]
, the element
[X]
is zero in
\mathbb{E}(C,A)
.
This shows 2).
∎

3.3.
Algebraic extriangulated categories

Proposition-Definition 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an extriangulated category. The following statements are equivalent:

1)

\mathcal{C}
is equivalent, as an extriangulated category, to a full extension-closed subcategory of an algebraic triangulated category.

2)

\mathcal{C}
is equivalent, as an extriangulated category, to
\mathcal{B}/(\mathcal{P}_{0})
for a Quillen exact category
\mathcal{B}
and a class
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of projective-injective objects.

3)

\mathcal{C}
is equivalent, as an extriangulated category, to
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
for an exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

If one of the above equivalent conditions holds, then
\mathcal{C}
is called an algebraic extriangulated category.
In this case, the exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
in 3) is called a dg enhancement for
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Let us prove that 1) implies 2). Suppose the extriangulated category
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is equivalent to a full extension-closed subcategory of
\underline{\mathcal{E}}
where
\mathcal{E}
is a Frobenius Quillen exact category.
Let
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{E}}
be the fully exact functor which is an equivalence onto its essential image.
Let
\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{E}}
be the canonical quotient functor.
We form the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathcal{E}}\mathrlap{\;.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Let
\mathcal{B}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{E}
consisting of objects
X
such that
\pi(X)
belongs to the essential image of
F
.
The category
\mathcal{B}
is extension-closed in
\mathcal{E}
since the essential image of
F
is closed under extension and
\pi
is a fully exact functor.
Note also that
\mathcal{B}
contains all projective-injective objects in
\mathcal{E}
.
So
\mathcal{B}
is canonically exact and the full subcategory

\mathcal{P}_{0}=\{P\in\mathcal{B}|\text{$P$ is projective-injective in $\mathcal{E}$}\}

consists of projective-injective objects of
\mathcal{B}
(but perhaps not all).
By
[
31
, Proposition 3.30]
,
\mathcal{B}/[\mathcal{P}_{0}]
has the structure of an extriangulated category, induced from that of
\mathcal{B}
.
The functor
F
induces a canonical equivalence of categories

G:\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{B}/[\mathcal{P}_{0}],\;\;A\mapsto F(A)

which, by definition, is a fully exact functor.

Let us prove that conversely, condition 2) implies 1). Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{B}/[\mathcal{P}_{0}]
as extriangulated categories.
Then we have a fully exact fully faithful functor

\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{B}/[\mathcal{P}_{0}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{B})/\mathrm{thick}(\mathcal{P}_{0}).

The triangle quotient
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{B})/\mathrm{thick}(\mathcal{P}_{0})
is an algebraic triangulated category and
\mathcal{C}
identifies with an extension-closed subcategory of
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{B})/\mathrm{thick}(\mathcal{P}_{0})
.

Let us show that 1) implies 3). Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to a full extension-closed subcategory of
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
for a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
Let
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
be a fully exact functor which is an equivalence onto its essential image.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
whose objects are those in the essential image of
F
.
Then
\mathcal{A}
is an extension-closed subcategory of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, since
F
is a fully exact functor.
By Example
2.16
6),
\mathcal{A}
inherits an exact dg structure from that of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
By the definition of the biadditive functor
\mathbb{E}
associated with an exact dg structure,
we see that the inclusion
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is a fully exact fully faithful functor.
Thus
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
as an extriangulated category where
\mathcal{A}
is an exact dg category.

Let us show that conversely, condition 3) implies 1).
Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
for an exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
.
By
[
9
, Remark 4.25]
, we may replace
\mathcal{A}
with
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
and assume that
\mathcal{A}
is a connective dg category.
Then by Theorem
3.1
, we have a fully exact fully embedding
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
where
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is an algebraic triangulated category.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

a)

By
[
9
, Remark 4.25]
, each algebraic extriangulated category admits a connective dg enhancement.

b)

Item 1) shows that the class of algebraic extriangulated categories is closed under forming extension-closed subcategories.

c)

Item 2) shows that the class of algebraic extriangulated categories is closed under forming ideal quotients by projective-injective objects.

d)

For an ideal quotient category
\mathcal{B}/(\mathcal{P}_{0})
, where
\mathcal{B}
is a Quillen exact category and
\mathcal{P}_{0}
is a class of projective-injective objects, it is a Frobenius extriangulated category
[
31
, Definition 7.1]
if and only if
\mathcal{B}
is a Frobenius exact category.
In particular, we have that the stable category of an algebraic Frobenius extriangulated category is an algebraic triangulated category.
Notice that although the stable category of a Frobenius extriangulated category is a triangulated category, it is not necessarily an algebraic triangulated category.

In fact, the class of extriangulated categories can also be described using a seemingly more
general version of item 2) in Proposition-Definition
3.14
.
This generalisation is suggested by
[
13
, Theorem 2.8]
,
stating that if
\mathcal{C}
is an extriangulated category and
\mathcal{I}
is an ideal generated by a family of morphisms

f:I\rightarrow P
from injectives to projectives, then
\mathcal{C}/\mathcal{I}
is naturally extriangulated.
In the context of algebraic extriangulated categories, we have the following result.

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an algebraic extriangulated category.
Let
\{f:I\rightarrow P\}
be a family of morphisms from injectives to projectives in
\mathcal{C}
.
Denote by
[I\rightarrow P]
the ideal generated by this family of morphisms.
Then
\mathcal{C}/[I\rightarrow P]
is still an algebraic extriangulated category.

Proof.

By item 2) of the preceding Proposition-Definition, we may assume
that
\mathcal{C}
is a Quillen exact category.
In
[
11
, Proposition 1.7]
, it is shown that given a full subcategory
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathcal{E}
of a Quillen exact category
\mathcal{E}
, the class of conflations which make the objects in
\mathcal{N}
projective-injective
is a Quillen exact structure on
\mathcal{E}
.
We endow
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
with the componentwise exact structure and view
\{f:I\rightarrow P\}
as a
full subcategory of
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be the exact category whose underlying category is
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
and whose exact structure is
obtained from
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})
by making the objects
f:I\rightarrow P
projective-injective.
Then
\mathcal{F}
is an exact category.
We have the inclusion functor

\theta:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

sending an object
X
to
(\mathrm{id}:X\rightarrow X)
. Clearly, it induces a functor

\varphi:\mathcal{C}/[I\rightarrow P]\rightarrow\mathcal{F}/(I\rightarrow P)
.
We have the following observations:

•

\theta
is an exact functor.
Indeed, a conflation in
\mathcal{C}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

is sent, by
\theta
, to a conflation in
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{C})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

For any morphism from
I\rightarrow P
to
\mathrm{id}:Z\rightarrow Z
, the morphism
P\rightarrow Z
factors through
Y
since
P
is projective in
\mathcal{C}
.
This shows that the morphism from
I\rightarrow P
to
\mathrm{id}:Z\rightarrow Z
factors through
\mathrm{id}:Y\rightarrow Y
.
Hence the objects
I\rightarrow P
are projective with respect to the conflations in the essential image of
\theta
.
Dually, one shows that they are injective.
Hence the functor
\theta
sends conflations to conflations in
\mathcal{F}
.

•

The induced functor
\varphi
is also fully faithful. This follows by definition.

•

The full embedding
\varphi:\mathcal{C}/[I\rightarrow P]\rightarrow\mathcal{F}/(I\rightarrow P)
is fully exact.
Let
\mathrm{id}_{X}:X\rightarrow X
and
\mathrm{id}_{Z}:Z\rightarrow Z
be two objects in the image of the functor
\varphi
.
Consider a conflation in
\mathcal{F}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\mathrlap{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

The map
\alpha
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}
.
Hence
\alpha:Y\rightarrow Y^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{id}:Y\rightarrow Y
and the above conflation is equivalent to a conflation in the essential image of
\theta
.

•

By definition, the category
\mathcal{F}/(I\rightarrow P)
is an algebraic extriangulated category.

Therefore, the functor
\varphi
is a fully exact fully faithful functor from
\mathcal{C}/[I\rightarrow P]
to an algebraic extriangulated category and hence
\mathcal{C}/[I\rightarrow P]
is also algebraic.
∎

3.4.
Higher extensions

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be a small extriangulated category.
In their paper
[
15
]
, for
n\geq 1
, Gorsky–Nakaoka–Palu defined the higher
extension bimodule
\mathbb{E}^{n}(?,-)
on
\mathcal{C}
as the
n
th tensor power over
\mathcal{C}
of
\mathbb{E}
.
Recall that a right
\mathcal{C}
-module
F:\mathcal{C}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}
is
weakly effaceable
if for each
C\in\mathcal{C}
and each element
x\in F(C)
, there exists a deflation
p:B\rightarrow C
such that
F(p)(x)=0
, cf.
[
23
, A.2]
and also
[
22
, Definition 2.6]
.
Dually one has the notion of weakly effaceable left module.
A
\mathcal{C}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodule
G:\mathcal{C}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}
is
weakly effaceable
, if for each

C\in\mathcal{C}
, the left module
G(C,-)
and the right module
G(?,C)
are both weakly effaceable.
By
[
31
, Corollary 3.5]
, for each
A\in\mathcal{C}
, the right
\mathcal{C}
-module

\mathbb{E}(?,A):\mathcal{C}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ab}
is weakly effaceable. It follows that the tensor powers

\mathbb{E}^{n}(?,-)
are weakly effaceable.

In the rest of this section, we will omit the symbol
∧
in the representable dg module
A^{\wedge}
for
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
Recall the extriangulated category
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
associated with the exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
.
Clearly
(\mathrm{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(?,-))_{n\geq 0}
together with the connecting morphisms given by triangles in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
induced by
\mathbb{E}
-triangles in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, is a
\delta
-functor (cf. Definition
2.5
) for
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
It clearly satisfies the assumptions
2.5.2
and
2.5.3
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a connective exact dg category and
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
the universal exact morphism from
\mathcal{A}

to a pretriangulated dg category.
Then we have a canonical isomorphism of
\delta
-functors
\alpha:\mathbb{E}^{n}(?,-)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(?,-)
.

Proof.

Put
\mathcal{C}=H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Let us show the
\mathcal{C}
-
\mathcal{C}
-bimodule
\mathrm{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(?,-)
is effaceable for each
n\geq 1
.
By construction, we have
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{N}
.
Consider a morphism from
C
to
\Sigma^{n}(A)
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
given by the roof

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}

where
s
and
w
are morphisms in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
and
N=\mathrm{Cone}(s)
lies in
\mathcal{N}
.
We may assume
N\in\mathcal{N}^{\leq 0}
.
By Corollary
3.12
,
N
is an extension of its homology which are defective objects in
\mathcal{H}
.
For defective objects
\widetilde{N}
we know that
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\widetilde{N},A^{\prime})
is concentrated in degree
2
for
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
.
So we may assume
N\in\mathcal{N}^{[-n+1,0]}
and write
N=(\Sigma^{n-1}N_{n-1})*(\Sigma^{n-2}N_{n-2})*\cdots*N_{0}
where
N_{i}
is defective for each
i
.
Suppose for each
i
,
N_{i}
is the totalization of a conflation
X_{i}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{i}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{i}}

We have a canonical map
p:N_{n-2}\rightarrow\Sigma^{2}N_{n-1}
.
Consider the following diagram in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma V_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}A_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}U_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}C_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{3}U_{n-1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Since
\mathcal{A}
is connective, we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(V_{n-2},\Sigma N_{n-1})=0
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(A_{n-2},\Sigma U_{n-1})=0
.
Thus we have a morphism
b:A_{n-2}\rightarrow C_{n-1}
which induces the morphism
p
.

The map from
\Sigma^{-1}N
to
\Sigma^{n}A
gives rise to a morphism
N_{n-1}\rightarrow\Sigma^{2}(A)
.
It induces a map
a:A_{n-1}\rightarrow A
which is well-defined up to a morphism factoring through
\overline{f}_{n-1}
.
Thus, we get an element
a_{*}[X_{n-1}]=\delta_{n-1}\in\mathbb{E}(C_{n-1},A)
.
We denote by
u
the composition
C\rightarrow N\rightarrow N_{\geq-n+2}
.
The map
\Sigma^{n-2}N_{n-2}\rightarrow N_{\geq-n+2}
gives rise to a canonical map
N_{\geq-n+2}\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}A_{n-2}
.
It induces a canonical map
v:\mathrm{Cone}(u)\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}A_{n-2}
.
We have the following commuative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}N_{\geq-n+2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-(n-1)}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}\mathrm{Cone}(u)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}N_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma V_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}

where
y
is the composition
\Sigma^{-n}\mathrm{Cone}(u)\rightarrow A_{n-2}\rightarrow C_{n-1}\rightarrow\Sigma V_{n-1}
.
Since the spaces
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma^{-n}N_{\geq-n+2},\Sigma A)
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma^{-n+1}C,\Sigma A)
both vanish, the space

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma^{-n}\mathrm{Cone}(u),\Sigma A)

also vanishes and hence the composition
\Sigma^{-n+1}M\xrightarrow{x}\Sigma A_{n-1}\xrightarrow{\Sigma a}\Sigma A
equals to the morphism
\Sigma^{-n+1}(w)
.
We have the following diagram in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n+1}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}\mathrm{Cone}(u)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma V_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n+1}C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma A.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n+1}(s)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n+1}(w)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n+1}(t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}(v)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma(a)}

Therefore, the morphism
w/s
is equal to the composition of
\Sigma^{-1}(v)/t
and a morphism
\Sigma^{n-1}A_{n-2}\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}A
.
Hence the left
\mathcal{C}
-module
\mathrm{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(C,-)
is effaceable for each
n\geq 1
and
C\in\mathcal{C}
.
Similarly one shows that the right
\mathcal{C}
-module
\mathrm{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A})}(?,A)
is effaceable for each
n\geq 1
and
A\in\mathcal{C}
.
The conclusion follows from Corollary
2.6
.
∎

3.5.
Quotients with respect to projective-injective objects

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a connective dg category and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
a full dg subcategory.
Put
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
Then for
A,B\in\mathcal{C}
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)=0

for
n>0
and

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,B)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{C})/[\mathcal{C}^{\prime}])(A,B).

Proof.

Put
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C})
.
Since
\mathcal{C}
is connective, by Proposition
2.9
we have a canonical co-t-structure
(\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0},\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0})
on
\mathcal{T}
, which restricts to a co-t-structure on
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
We denote by
\mathcal{T}^{-n}
the
\Sigma^{n}
of the coheart of the co-t-structure.

Suppose we have a morphism in
\mathcal{B}
as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n}B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

where
s
and
b
are morphisms in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C})
and
P=\mathrm{Cone}(s)
lies in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
Take a weight decomposition of
P:\sigma_{>-1}(P)\rightarrow P\rightarrow\sigma_{\leq-1}P\rightarrow\Sigma\sigma_{\geq-1}P
.
Since
A\in H^{0}(\mathcal{C})
lies in the coheart of the co-t-structure,
A\rightarrow P
factors through
\sigma_{>-1}(P)
.
Hence we may assume
P\in\mathcal{T}^{>-1}
.
Note that for
n\geq 0
we have
\Sigma^{n}B\in\mathcal{T}^{-n}
and hence the map
\Sigma^{-1}P\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}B
vanishes.
Therefore, the map
b
factors through
A
for
n\geq 0
.
So for
n>0
, we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)=0
.

Now assume
n=0
.
If a map
f:A\rightarrow B
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C})
factors through an object in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, then it factors through an object in the coheart, i.e., a retract of an object
W\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.
Therefore, it factors through an object in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
So we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,B)=(H^{0}(\mathcal{C})/[\mathcal{C}^{\prime}])(A,B).

∎

Definition 3.19
.

An object
P
in
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is said to be projective if for each conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

the morphism
\overline{\jmath}
induces a surjection
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(P,Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(P,Z)
, or equivalently, if
P
is projective in the extriangulated category
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.
Dually, we define injective objects in
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a connective exact dg category.
Then an object
P
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is projective if and only if
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}(P,\mathcal{N})=0
where
\mathcal{N}
is the full triangulated subcategory of
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
generated by the total dg modules of conflations.

Proof.

Notice that the totalization
N
of a conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

lies in the heart of the canonical t-structure of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
and is the cokernel of the map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(-,Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(-,Z).

Also for objects
A^{\prime}
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}(A^{\prime},\Sigma^{i}N)=0
for
i\neq 0
.
So
P
is projective if and only if
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(P,N)=0
for each totalization
N
of a conflation.
∎

Let
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be the universal exact morphism from a connective exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
into a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
Recall that we put
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})=H^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
.
Let
(\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0},\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0})
be the canonical co-t-structure on
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
introduced in Proposition
2.9
.
Since we have

\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}\mathcal{H}[-i]*\mathcal{H}[-i+1]*\cdots*\mathcal{H}

where
\mathcal{H}
is the coheart of the co-t-structure consisting of direct summands of
A
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
for
A\in\mathcal{A}
, it follows from Theorem
3.1
that
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A,M)
is quasi-isomorphic to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,M)
for
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
M\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
.
If
P
is a projective object in the extriangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, then by Lemma
3.20

P
lies in the left orthogonal of
\mathcal{N}
and we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}(P,X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(FP,X)

for any
X\in\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
consisting of projective-injective objects in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Then for
A,B\in\mathcal{A}
, the following statements hold:

1)

The canonical map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
is bijective for
n\leq 0
.

2)

The canonical map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
is bijective for
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Consider a morphism in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n}B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

where
s
and
b
are morphisms in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
P=\mathrm{Cone}(s)\in\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Put
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
. Since objects in
\mathcal{P}
are injective, the quotient functor
F:\mathcal{T}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{N}
induces bijections
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(X,Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(X,Q)
for
X\in\mathcal{T}
and
Q\in\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
. We may assume
s=F(s^{\prime})
for some morphism
s^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}
.
Let
(\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0},\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0})
be the canonical co-t-structure on
\mathcal{T}
.
Since
A\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
, we may assume
P\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
.

1) Assume
n\leq 0
. Consider the triangle
\Sigma^{-1}P\rightarrow M\xrightarrow{s}A\rightarrow P
in
\mathcal{T}
and the induced long exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(M,\Sigma^{-1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(P,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(A,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}(M,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M,\Sigma^{-1}B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M,B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the Hom spaces in the second row are in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
By the Five-Lemma, we have

(3.21.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(M,\Sigma^{n}B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(M,\Sigma^{n}B)

for
n\leq 0
.
So we have
b=F(b^{\prime})
for some morphism
b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}
.
This shows that the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)

is a surjection for
n\leq 0
.
Assume that the morphism
b=F(b^{\prime}):M\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}B
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
factors through an object in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
By bijection (
3.21.1
) and since
\mathcal{P}
consists of projective-injective objects, the morphism
b^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{T}
also factors through an object in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
So the canonical map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
is a bijection for each
n\leq 0
.

2) Assume
n\geq 1
.
Since
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(\Sigma^{-1}P,\Sigma^{n}B)=0
for
P\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
, the morphism
b:M\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}B
factors through
A
.
So the map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
is a surjection.
Let
f:A\rightarrow\Sigma^{n}B
be a morphism in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
which factors through an object
Q
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
We may assume
Q\in\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})^{\geq 0}
.
Then we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(Q,\Sigma^{n}B)=0
and hence the morphism
f
is zero.
So the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)

is a bijection for
n\geq 1
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a full dg subcategory of a connective exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
consisting of projective-injective objects.
Then we have the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathcal{A})/[H^{0}(\mathcal{P})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}}

In summary, we have

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
A,B\in\mathcal{A}
. The following statements hold:

1)

H
is fully faithful.

2)

For
n\geq 1
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)=0
.

3)

For
n\leq 0
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
.

4)

For
n\geq 1
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
.
In particular, the essential image of
F_{2}\circ H
is closed under extension in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.

5)

For
n\leq 0
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}(A,\Sigma^{n}B)
.

For an extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}
with a subcategory
\mathcal{P}
consisting of (not necessarily all) projective-injective objects in
\mathcal{C}
, Nakaoka–Palu showed that the ideal quotient
\mathcal{C}/[\mathcal{P}]
has the structure of an extriangulated category, induced from that of
\mathcal{C}
, cf.
[
31
, Proposition 3.30]
. In the context of exact dg categories, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 3.23
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a connective exact dg category and
\mathcal{P}
a full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
consisting of projective-injective objects in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the canonical dg enhancement of
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Then the dg quotient
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
carries a canonical exact structure
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
induced from
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and its dg derived category is quasi-equivalent to
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Proof.

By the items
(1)
and
(3)
of Proposition
3.22
, we have
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P})=H^{0}(\mathcal{A})/[\mathcal{P}]
and the canonical morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
is quasi-fully faithful.
By item (4), the quasi-essential image of
\varphi
is closed under extensions in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
and therefore
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
carries a canonical exact dg structure.
Again by item
(4)
and Proposition
3.17
, the bounded dg derived category of
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
is quasi-equivalent to
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.
∎

In particular, we obtain a short exact sequence of triangulated categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P},\overline{\mathcal{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Remark 3.24
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
be an algebraic extriangulated category.
By Proposition-Definition
3.14
we have that
\mathcal{E}
is equivalent, as an extriangulated category, to
\mathcal{B}/(\mathcal{P}_{0})
for a Quillen exact category
\mathcal{B}
and a class
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of projective-injective objects.
Then by Proposition
3.22
, we could take the dg quotient
\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{P}_{0}
as a dg enhancement of
(\mathcal{E},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
.

Corollary 3.25
.

Each connective exact dg category is exactly quasi-equivalent to a dg quotient
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{P}_{0}
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a Quillen exact category and
\mathcal{P}_{0}
is a full subcategory of projective-injectives in
\mathcal{E}
, with the exact dg structure in Theorem
3.23
. In particular, each connective additive dg category is quasi-equivalent to a dg quotient
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{P}_{0}
of an additive category
\mathcal{E}
by a full subcategory
\mathcal{P}_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a connective exact dg category.
By Theorem
3.1
, we have a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{A}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is an extension-closed subcategory of a (strictly) pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{b}(\mathcal{A})
.
Then
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D})
is a Frobenius exact category with the contractible objects being the projective-injectives, when endowed with the graded-split exact structure.
Its stable category is isomorphic to the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{D})
.
Note that
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{Z^{0}(\mathcal{D})}(?,-)
of this exact structure is naturally isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{D})}(?,\Sigma(-))
.
Since
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
is extension-closed in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D})
, it inherits a Quillen exact structure with respect to which all the contractibles are projective-injective.
We denote by
\mathrm{ctr}(\mathcal{D})\subset Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
the full subcategory consisting of all the contractibles in
\mathcal{D}
.
We have the following diagram in
\mathrm{Hqe}

\hbox to420.55pt{\vbox to51.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 210.27092pt\lower-25.75394pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}}{
}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -234.78 -27.11 L -154.91 -27.11}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-169.6727pt}{-19.59421pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(-1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -234.78 -27.11)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 2.71 C 0.95 2.71 1.72 2.1 1.72 1.36 C 1.72 0.61 0.95 0 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-111.75421pt}{-19.59421pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -154.63 -27.11)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Put
\mathcal{T}=H^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))/\mathrm{ctr}(\mathcal{D}))
.
By Proposition
3.17
and Proposition
3.22
, the morphism
F
induces a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{T}}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{D})}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)

for
X
and
Y
in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
and
i\geq 1
.
Let us show that it is also true for
i\leq 0
.
For this we work with
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathrm{tr}(Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))/\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{ctr}(\mathcal{D}))
instead of
\mathcal{T}
(and the morphism
G
instead of
F
), by Proposition
3.22
(3).
Suppose we have a morphism
f:\Sigma^{-i}X\rightarrow Y
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{D})
for
i\leq 0
.
Recall that we denote by
IX=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{X})
.
We have the following sequence in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces IX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{2}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{3}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{-i}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{2}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-i}X}

The morphism
f:\Sigma^{-i}X\rightarrow Y
is represented by a cocylce
g:\Sigma^{-i}X\rightarrow Y
.
Then
g
together with the projection of the above sequence to
X
yields a morphism
\Sigma^{-i}X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
It is clear that its image under the functor
H^{0}(G):\mathcal{T}\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{D})
is
f
.

Suppose we have a morphism
X\rightarrow\Sigma^{i}Y

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{i}Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
whose image under
H^{0}(G)
is zero in
H^{0}(\mathcal{D})
.
We may assume that
Z
is of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces IX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{2}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{-i}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}

where
X
is in degree zero, and that the morphism
s:Z\rightarrow X
is given by the projection onto
X
.
Then the morphism
a
is induced by a morphism
I^{-i}X\rightarrow Y

whose precomposition with
I^{-i-1}X\rightarrow I^{-i}X
is zero.
So it induces a cocyle
\Sigma^{-i}X\rightarrow Y
which by assumption is a coboundary.
Hence the morphism
a
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))
factors through
I^{-i+1}X
and the morphism
a/s:X\rightarrow\Sigma^{i}Y
is zero in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.

Since the objects in
D^{\prime}
form a set of generators for the triangulated categories
\mathcal{T}
and
H^{0}(\mathcal{D})
, it follows that
F
is a quasi-equivalence.
It follows that
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is quasi-equivalent to the dg quotient
Z^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})/\mathrm{ctr}(\mathcal{D})
.
For the second statement, we simply notice that each additive dg category
admits the trivial exact structure given by the closure under isomorphisms of the class of all split exact sequences.
∎

3.6.
Examples

We illustrate the results in this section through the following classes of examples.

3.6.1.
Stable dg categories

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a small connective exact dg category such that the associated extriangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is triangulated.
By
[
9
, Theorem 6.5]
,
\mathcal{A}
is a stable dg category with the canonical exact structure
\mathcal{S}
.
We show that
\mathcal{A}
is quasi-equivalent to the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of a pretriangulated dg category.

Proposition 3.26
.

The connective dg category
\mathcal{A}
is quasi-equivalent to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

Proof.

The canonical functor
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})=H^{0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))
is a triangle functor and it is fully faithful by Theorem
3.1
.
Its essential image contains a family of generators for
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and hence it a triangle equivalence.
By Theorem
3.1
, we have that
\mathcal{A}
is quasi-equivalent to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
∎

Similarly, one shows that if
\mathcal{A}
is the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of a pretriangulated dg category
\mathcal{T}
with the induced exact structure, then
\mathcal{T}
is the bounded dg derived category of
\mathcal{A}
via the canonical dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
.

Corollary 3.27
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a connective Frobenius exact dg category, i.e., the associated extriangulated category
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is Frobenius.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be its full dg subcategory of projectives.
Endow
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}
with the exact structure
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
given by Theorem
3.23
.
Then we have an exact quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P}))
.

Proof.

Since
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P})=H^{0}(\mathcal{A})/[H^{0}(\mathcal{P})]
is a triangulated category,
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P},\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})
is a stable dg category with the canonical exact structure.
By Proposition prop:stable and Theorem
3.23
, we have

\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{P},\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})/\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})).

∎

3.6.2.
Cohen–Macaulay dg modules

This example is taken from
[
21
]
.
Let
k
be a field.
A dg
k
-algebra
A
is
proper
if
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\dim_{k}{H^{i}(A)<\infty}
.
It is
Gorenstein
if the thick subcategory
\mathrm{per}(A)
of the derived category
\mathcal{D}(A)

generated by
A
coincides with the thick subcategory
\mathrm{thick}(DA)
generated by
DA
, where
D=\mathrm{Hom}_{k}(-,k)
is the
k\mbox{-dual}
.
Let
A
be a connective proper Gorenstein dg algebra.
A dg
A\mbox{-}
module is
perfectly valued
if its total cohomology is finite-dimensional and we denote by
\mathrm{pvd}(A)
the triangulated category of perfectly valued dg
A\mbox{-}
modules.
A dg
A\mbox{-}
module
M
in
\mathrm{pvd}(A)
is
Cohen–Macaulay
if
H^{i}(M)=0
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(A)}(M,\Sigma^{i}A)=0
for
i>0
.
Let
\mathrm{CM}A
be the subcategory of
\mathrm{pvd}(A)
consisting of Cohen-Macaulay dg
A\mbox{-}
modules.
By
[
21
, Theorem 2.4]
, the category
\mathrm{CM}A
is an
\mathrm{Ext}\mbox{-}
finite Frobenius extriangulated category with
\mathrm{proj}\,(\mathrm{CM}A)=\mathrm{add}A
.
Let
\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
be the canonical dg enhancement of
\mathrm{pvd}(A)
and
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathrm{pvd}(A)
the full dg subcategory consisting of Cohen–Macaulay dg modules.
Put
\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)=\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
.
Since
\mathcal{A}
is extension-closed in
\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
, the dg category
\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
inherits a canonical exact structure
\mathcal{S}
whose corresponding extriangulated category is
\mathrm{CM}A
.

Proposition 3.28
.

The bounded dg derived category of
(\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A),\mathcal{S})
is quasi-equivalent to
\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
.

Proof.

Clearly, the canonical dg functor
\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)\rightarrow\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
is exact.
By Theorem
3.1
, it induces a canonical morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A),\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
.
For objects
X
and
Y
in
\mathrm{CM}(A)
, we have a canonical bijection
\mathbb{E}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pvd}(A)}(X,\Sigma Y)
.
Note that for projective objects
P\in\mathrm{add}(A)
, the Hom spaces
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(A)}(P,\Sigma^{i}M)
vanish for
i>0
and
M\in\mathrm{CM}A
, by the definition of Cohen–Macaulay dg modules.
Since the exact dg category
\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
has enough projectives, we have bijections
\mathbb{E}^{i}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pvd}(A)}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)
for
i\geq 1
.
By
[
21
, Lemma 3.9 (2)]
, the triangulated category
\mathrm{pvd}(A)
is generated by
\mathrm{CM}A
.
Therefore the above morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A),\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{pvd}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
is a quasi-equivalence.
∎

This equivalence induces an equivalence from the singularity category of
\mathrm{CM}_{\mathrm{dg}}(A)
onto that of
A
in complete analogy with
the situation for a finite-dimensional Iwanaga–Gorenstein algebra concentrated in degree

0
.

3.6.3.
t-structures

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a pretriangulated dg category and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
a full dg subcategory such that
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is an aisle in the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{T})
.
Clearly
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is stable under extensions in
\mathcal{T}
and hence inherits a canonical exact structure
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S})
, cf. Example
2.16
6).
Put
\mathcal{A}=\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
By
[
9
, Remark 4.5 a)]
, we obtain a canonical exact structure
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
The composite dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}
is exact and hence induces a canonical morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
.

Proposition 3.29
.

The canonical morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
is a quasi-equivalence.

Proof.

Let
X
be an object in
\mathcal{A}
.
We view it as an object in the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{T})
.
We have the triangle
X\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\Sigma X\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma X}}\Sigma X
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Since
\Sigma X
is also an object in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, this means that
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is an extriangulated category with enough injectives and the injectives are the zero objects.
Now, the same reasoning as in the proof of Proposition
3.28
shows that the morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
is a quasi-equivalence.
∎

3.6.4.
Relative cluster categories as derived categories

One of the motivations for our work is Yilin Wu’s construction of the Higgs category associated with a finite ice quiver with potential (see
[
40
]
[
39
]
for the Jacobi-finite case and
[
27
]
for the Jacobi-infinite case).

Let
(Q,F,W)
be a Jacobi-finite ice quiver with potential.
Denote by
\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
the relative Ginzburg dg algebra
\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}(Q,F,W)
.
Let
e=\sum_{i\in F}e_{i}
be the idempotent associated with all frozen vertices.
Let
\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
of the dg
\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
-modules whose restriction to frozen vertices is acyclic.
Then the
relative cluster category

\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}(Q,F,W)
associated to
(Q,F,W)
is defined as the Verdier quotient of triangulated categories

\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})/\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}).

The
relative fundamental domain

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
associated to
(Q,F,W)
is defined as the following subcategory of
\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})

\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}{\coloneqq}\{\mathrm{Cone}(X_{1}\xrightarrow{f}X_{0})|X_{i}\in\mathrm{add}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\text{ and }\mathrm{Hom}(f,I)\text{ is surjective},\forall I\in\mathcal{P}{\coloneqq}\mathrm{add}(e\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\}.

It is clear that for objects
X
and
Y
in
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
, every morphism
f:X\rightarrow\Sigma Y
in
\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
factors through an object in
\mathrm{add}(\Sigma\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
.
Let
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}:\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
be the canonical quotient functor.
By
[
40
, Proposition 5.20]
, the restriction of the quotient functor
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}
to
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
is fully faithful.
In particular for objects
X
and
Y
in
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
, the functor
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}
induces an injection

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})}(X,Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}X,\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}Y).

The
Higgs category
(
[
40
, Definition 5.22]
)
\mathcal{H}
is the image of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
in
\mathcal{C}
under the quotient functor
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}
.
Indeed, we have the following

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
X,Y
be objects in
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
.
Then
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}
induces a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}X,\Sigma^{i}\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}Y).

for any
i\leq 0
or for
Y\in\mathrm{add}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
and
i\leq 1
.

Proof.

We first show the case when
Y=\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
and
i\leq 1
.
Recall the relative t-structure
(\mathcal{D}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\geq 0})
on
\mathcal{D}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
which restricts to t-structures on
\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
and
\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
, cf.
[
40
, Propositions 4.10, 4.11]
.
We show the injectivity of the map.
Let
f:X\rightarrow\Sigma^{i}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
be a map in
\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
which factors through an object
M
in
\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
.
We want to show that
f
is indeed a zero map.
By definition, we have
\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\in\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0}
and hence
X\in\mathrm{add}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}*\mathrm{add}\Sigma\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}\subset\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0}
.
So we may suppose
M\in\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0}
.
By
[
40
, Corollary 3.13]
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}}(M,\Sigma^{i}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\xrightarrow{\sim}D\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}},\Sigma^{3-i}M)=0

where
D=\mathrm{Hom}(-,k)
.
This shows the injectivity of the morphism.
We now show the surjectivity of the map. Consider a roof
b/s

\hbox to148.28pt{\vbox to49.23pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 74.13676pt\lower-24.61655pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-74.13676pt}{-19.45682pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -102.58 -26.92)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{20.98814pt}{3.81654pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 29.04 5.28)} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Since
X\in\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0}
, we may assume
\mathrm{Cone}(s)\in\mathrm{pvd}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\leq 0}
.
Then since we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}}(\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(s),\Sigma^{i}\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}D\mathrm{Hom}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}}(\Gamma,\Sigma^{2-i}\mathrm{Cone}(s))=0,

the morphism
b
factors through
s
and this shows the surjectivity of the map.

For general
Y\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
and
i\leq 0
, it follows from the above case by applying the homological functor
\mathrm{Hom}(X,-)
to the defining triangle for
Y
.
∎

Let
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
respectively
\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the canonical dg enhancement of
\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
respectively
\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
.
Let
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the canonical dg enhancement of
\mathcal{C}
given by the dg quotient
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})/\mathrm{pvd}_{e}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})_{\mathrm{dg}}
.
Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
consisting of objects in
\mathcal{H}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}
be the dg full subcategory of
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
consisting of objects in
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
.
By
[
40
, Definition 5.12, Proposition 5.39]
, the dg category
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
) is extension-closed in
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
(resp.,
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
) and thus inherits a canonical exact dg structure.
By Lemma
3.30
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 3.31
.

The canonical morphism
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
induces a quasi-equivalence

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{rel}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Proposition 3.32
.

Via the canonical quasi-equivalence
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
, the exact dg structure on
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}
is identified with the exact substructure on
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
which makes the objects in
\mathrm{add}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
projective.
Moreover, the bounded dg derived category of
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
is
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
and
the bounded dg derived category of
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{rel}}
is
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
.

Proof.

Let
X
and
Y
be two objects in
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
and
g:\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}X\rightarrow\Sigma\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}Y
a morphism in
\mathcal{C}
.
Then by Lemma
3.30
,
g
is of the form
\pi^{\mathrm{rel}}(f)
for some
f:X\rightarrow\Sigma Y
if and only if
g
factors though an object in
\mathrm{add}(\pi\Sigma\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
.
This is exactly the exact substructure which makes the objects in
\mathrm{add}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
projective.

By
[
40
, Section 5.8]
, Theorem
3.1
and Proposition
3.17
, the canonical exact morphism

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}

characterises
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}
as the bounded dg derived category of
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Since
\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}}
is projective in the extriangulated category
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
, higher extensions
\mathbb{E}^{i}
of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
vanish for
i\geq 2
.
Also, we have
\mathrm{Ext}^{i}_{\mathrm{per}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})}(X,Y)=0
for
X,Y\in\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}
and
i\geq 2
.
Thus by Theorem
3.1
and Proposition
3.17
, the canonical exact morphism

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})

characterises
\mathrm{per}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{rel}})
as the bounded dg derived category of
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{rel}}_{\mathrm{dg}}
.

∎

4.
Reproduction of exact dg categories

In this section, we show that “functor dg categories”
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
with exact target
\mathcal{A}
and connective source
\mathcal{B}
carry canonical exact structures, cf. Theorem
4.1
.
As an example, we show that when
\mathcal{B}
is a certain path
k
-category with radical square zero relations, and
\mathcal{A}
carries the trivial exact structure, we obtain a Frobenius exact structure, cf. Proposition
4.7
.
We define
biexact
morphisms from the tensor product of two exact dg categories to another exact dg category and show the existence of a universal biexact morphism.
We show that in this way we obtain a closed monoidal category with internal hom whose value on a pair of small connective exact dg categories
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
given by extension-closed dg subcategory of
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
consisting of exact morphisms.

In this section, it is convenient to work with
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
instead of
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
, to be consistent with
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
Recall that by
[
9
, 3.2]
, we have a fully faithful functor
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
whose essential image consists of the squares in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

(4.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

where
N
is acyclic.
In particular, for such squares (
4.0.1
), we may assume that
X
,
Y
,
Z
are representable dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules and
N=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{X})
.
An exact dg structure on an additive dg category
\mathcal{A}
can then be defined to be a class
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
of homotopy bicartesian squares (
4.0.1
), where
N
is acyclic, which satisfies similar axioms as those of Definition
2.14
.
Exceptionally, we will employ this definition in this section.

4.1.
Functor dg categories

A famous slogan by Amnon Neeman is: triangulated categories do not reproduce.
A key feature of exact dg categories is that they do reproduce, as the following theorem shows.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a small exact dg category.
If
\mathcal{B}
is a small connective dg category, then
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is canonically an exact dg category.

We will prove Theorem
4.1
after Lemma
4.3
.
For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of cofibrant quasi-representable dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules.
The Yoneda dg functor
Y:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})
induces a quasi-equivalence from
\mathcal{A}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
, which we still denote by
Y
.
We may assume that
\mathcal{B}
is cofibrant as a dg
k
-category.
For each
B\in\mathcal{B}
, let
R_{B}:\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
be the dg functor given by
X\mapsto X(-,B)
.
We have a morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}
given by the following roof of dg functors

It then induces a functor
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathcal{I},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}(k\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A})
for each small category
\mathcal{I}
, which we denote by
F^{\mathcal{I}}_{B}
or
F_{B}
if there is no ambiguity.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
X
be an object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))
.
If for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
,
F_{B}(X)\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
is a homotopy cocartesian (resp., cartesian) square,
then
X
is also a homotopy cocartesian (resp., cartesian) square.

Proof.

We show the case when
F_{B}(X)
is homotopy cocartesian for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
. The other case can be shown similarly.
We may suppose that
X
is of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces IX_{0}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\wedge}}

where
X_{i}\in\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
for
i=0,1,2
, and
IX_{0}^{\wedge}=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{X_{0}^{\wedge}})
.

in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
, we denote by

its cone.

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Let
W
be an object in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
Since
F_{B}(X)
is homotopy cocartesian for
B\in\mathcal{B}
, for each pair of objects
B_{1},B_{2}
in
\mathcal{B}
, and
i\leq 0
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(X_{2}(-,B_{1}),\Sigma^{i}W(-,B_{2}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(C(f)(-,B_{1}),\Sigma^{i}W(-,B_{2})),

and hence for
j\leq 0
we have

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X_{2},\Sigma^{j}W)\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(C(f),\Sigma^{j}W).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))}(X_{2}^{\wedge},\Sigma^{j}W^{\wedge})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})}(X_{2},\Sigma^{j}W)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X_{2},\Sigma^{j}W))

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X_{2},\Sigma^{j}W))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(C(f),\Sigma^{j}W))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(C(f),\Sigma^{j}W))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})}(C(f),\Sigma^{j}W)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))}(C(f^{\wedge}),\Sigma^{j}W^{\wedge}).

This shows that
X
is a homotopy cocartesian square.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
S
be an object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))
(resp.,
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sp},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))
).
If for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
, the object
F_{B}(S)
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
(resp.,
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sp},\mathcal{A})
) admits a homotopy pullback (resp., homotopy pushout).
Then
S
admits a homotopy pullback (resp., homotopy pushout).

Proof.

We show the case when
S\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))
.
The other case can be shown dually.
We may assume
S
is of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PX_{2}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\wedge}}

where
PX_{2}^{\wedge}=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}X_{2}^{\wedge}})
, and
X_{i}\in\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
,
i=1
,
2
, and
j
is a graded-split surjection.
Since
j
and
PX_{2}\rightarrow X_{2}
are graded-split surjections, by assumption,
for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
,
F_{B}(S)
admits a homotopy pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PX_{2}(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}(-,B)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{(-,B)}}

where
H(B)\in H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Let
V
be the kernel of the map
X_{1}\oplus PX_{2}\rightarrow X_{2}
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
.
Then for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
, we have a canonical map
s_{B}:H(B)\rightarrow V(-,B)
.
Put
G(B)=\Sigma\mathrm{Cone}(s_{B})
. By definition
G(B)
lies in the heart of the canonical t-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
,
which is equivalent to
\mathrm{Mod}\,(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))
.
For each morphism
b:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
, we have a canonical morphism
H(b):H(B)\rightarrow H(B^{\prime})
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
which makes the following diagram in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-,B^{\prime}).}

Since
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma H(B),\Sigma^{-1}G(B^{\prime}))=0
, it induces a unique morphism
G(b):G(B)\rightarrow G(B^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
rendering the following commutative diagram in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}G(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma H(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-,B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}G(B^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma H(B^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(b)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V(-{,}b)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}G(b)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma H(b)}

Therefore we have a canonical functor
G:H^{0}(\mathcal{B})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}\,(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))
which sends
B
to
G(B)
and
b:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
to
G(b):G(B)\rightarrow G(B^{\prime})
.
By adjunction, the functor
G
corresponds to an object in
\mathrm{Mod}\,(H^{0}(\mathcal{B})^{op}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{A}))
, which is equivalent to the heart of the canonical t-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
.
We still denote by
G
the corresponding object in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
. We may assume
G
is cofibrant.

Next, we will define a morphism from
\Sigma V
to
G
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
.
We have the canonical isomorphism

(41.1)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma V,G)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma V,G)).

We have that
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma V,G)
is concentrated in degrees 0 and 1.
So we have

(41.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma V,G))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{(H^{0}\mathcal{B})^{e}}(H^{0}(\mathcal{B}),H^{0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Sigma V,G)).

For each pair of objects
B,B^{\prime}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
, we have the map

\alpha_{B,B^{\prime}}:\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{B})}(B,B^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma V(-,B),G(-,B^{\prime}))

where
b:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
is sent to
\Sigma V(-,B)\rightarrow G(-,B)\xrightarrow{G(-,b)}G(-,B^{\prime})
where the first map is the canonical map.
It is not hard to check

\alpha_{-,-}:\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{B})}(-,-)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(\Sigma V,G)

defines an
H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
-
H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
-bimodule morphism.
By the canonical isomorphisms (
41.1
) and (
41.2
), the morphism
\alpha_{-,-}
defines a morphism
u:\Sigma V\rightarrow G
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{A})
. By definition,
u_{(-,B)}
is the canonical morphism
\Sigma V(-,B)\rightarrow G(-,B)
.

Put
X_{0}=\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(u)
.
Then
X_{0}(-,B)
is quasi-isomorphic to
H(B)
for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
and hence
X_{0}\in\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
Consider the composition morphism
X_{0}\rightarrow V\rightarrow X_{1}\oplus PX_{2}
.
The sequence
X_{0}\rightarrow X_{1}\oplus PX_{2}\rightarrow X_{2}
then corresponds to a square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PX_{2}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}^{\wedge}}

which is homotopy cartesian and hence a homotopy pullback of
S
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
be the class of homotopy bicartesian squares
X
in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
such that
F_{B}(X)\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
is a conflation for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
.
By Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
, one checks that
(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})
is an exact dg category.
∎

In Theorem
4.1
, when
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are concentrated in degree zero so that
\mathcal{A}
is a Quillen exact category by Example
2.16
2), we recover the pointwise exact structure on the category
\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
of
k
-linear functors.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a
k
-category and
\mathcal{A}
a Quillen exact category considered as an exact dg category.
Then
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is concentrated in degree zero.
Moreover, we have a canonical equivalence of exact categories
\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, where
\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is endowed with the pointwise exact structure, and
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is endowed with the exact structure from Theorem
4.1
.

Proof.

For objects
X
and
Y
in
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op})}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B}^{e})}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)).

Note that the space
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,\Sigma^{i}Y)
is zero if
i<0
, since the dg category
\mathcal{A}
is concentrated in degree
0
.
Thus we have a quasi-equivalence of dg categories

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}H^{0}(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}).

Therefore, the canonical exact dg structure on
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
provided by Theorem
4.1
induces a Quillen exact structure on
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.

The heart of the canonical t-structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op})
is equivalent to
\mathrm{Mod}\,(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op})
.
Since the objects
X
and
Y
both have cohomology concentrated in degree 0, they belong to the heart.
So they correspond to
k
-linear functors
\mathcal{A}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}\,k
.
Furthermore, they are given by functors
F,G:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
because
X(-,B)
and
Y(-,B)
are quasi-representable for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
.
So we have an inclusion functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{Mod}\,\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{A}^{op}\otimes\mathcal{B},\mathrm{Mod}\,k)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Mod}\,(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op})

whose essential image is exactly
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
If we identify the categories
\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
and
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
via the above functor, we see that the Quillen exact structure on
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is identified with the componentwise Quillen exact structure on
\mathrm{Fun}_{k}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

From the definition of the canonical exact dg structure on
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, it is clear that an exact morphism
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
induces an exact morphism
F_{*}:\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
.

Remark 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a connective dg category.
Let
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be the universal exact morphism from
\mathcal{A}
to a pretriangulated dg category.
By Remark
4.5
, the canonical morphism

F_{*}:\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))

is exact and hence induces a canonical morphism

\varphi:\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}))

in
\mathrm{Hqe}
.
For objects
X
and
Y
in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, the canonical map

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(F_{*}X,F_{*}Y).

is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(k)
.
Also, by Corollary
2.13
, the canonical map

(4.6.1)

\mathbb{E}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}))}(F_{*}X,\Sigma F_{*}Y)

is a bijection.
We do not know whether the same is true for higher extension groups (and hence do not know whether
H^{0}(\varphi)
is a triangle equivalence).

We end this subsection with an example which generalises the category of
2
-periodic complexes over an additive category.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be the path
k
-category of the following quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

with relations
uv=0
and
vu=0
.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a connective additive dg category with the trivial exact structure
\mathcal{S}
, cf. Example
2.16
5).

Proposition 4.7
.

The dg category
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
with the exact structure
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
given by Theorem
4.1
is Frobenius.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
