{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{X}}
are also projective-injective in
(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})
.
For each object
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, we have a conflation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\oplus Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\oplus Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\oplus Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}f{]^{\intercal}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\ 0\\
0\ 1\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-f{,}1{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}g{,}1{]^{\intercal}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}1\ 0\\
0\ 0\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-1{,}g{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}1{,}f{]^{\intercal}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-f{,}1{]}}

Hence the exact dg category
(\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mathcal{S}})
has enough injectives. Dually, it also has enough projectives and therefore it is a Frobenius exact dg category.
∎

4.2.
Closed monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Hqe}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathrm{ex}}

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
be small exact dg categories.
Recall that a morphism
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
is exact if the induced functor
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
sends objects in
\mathcal{S}
to objects in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
.
Recall that we denote by
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}((\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}),(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}))
the subset of
\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
consisiting of exact morphisms.
Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime})
be an exact dg category.
A morphism
\mu:\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
is
biexact
, if for each object
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, the induced morphisms

\mu_{A,-}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{C},\;\;\mu_{-,A^{\prime}}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}

are both exact morphisms.
Suppose
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
are both connective.
By Theorem
3.1
, we have universal fully exact embeddings
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
into pretriangulated dg categories.
We denote by
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
the full dg subcategory of
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
consisting of the exact morphisms.

Lemma 4.8
.

Suppose
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
are small connective exact dg categories.
The dg category
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is stable under extensions in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
.
In particular, it inherits a canonical exact dg structure.

Proof.

Suppose we have a conflation in
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})

F\rightarrow G\rightarrow H

where we omit the homotopy, and where
F
and
H
are exact morphisms.
We would like to show that
G
is also exact.
Assume that we are given a conflation in
\mathcal{A}

A\rightarrow B\rightarrow C

where again we omit the homotopy.
Then we have the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})

(4.8.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(-,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(-,C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(-,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(-,C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(-,A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(-,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H(-,C)}

where we have omitted the homotopies.
The totalizations of the first and the third rows are in
\mathcal{N}
associated with
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
, cf. Subsection
3.2
.
The totalization of the second row, being an extension of the totalizations of the first
and the third rows, is thus also in
\mathcal{N}
.
Therefore the second row is homotopy bicartesian in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.
Consider the image of the diagram (
4.8.1
) under the universal exact morphism
F:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
.
By Theorem
3.1
, we see that the image of the second row under
F
is isomorphic to the image of a conflation under
F
.
Therefore, the second row in (
4.8.1
) is a conflation.
It follows that
G
is exact.
∎

Let
\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of the extension closure of the quasi-essential image of the morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}

F:\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}))

with the inherited exact structure.

Proposition 4.9
.

The natural morphism
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is the universal biexact morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}
from
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
to an exact dg category.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime})
be an exact dg category and
G:\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
a biexact morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}
.
Since
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is connective, the morphism
G
factors through
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C}
.
We still denote the induced exact structure on
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C}
by
\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}
.
By adunction, we obtain a morphism
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}))
.
We endow
\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}))
with the exact structure given by Lemma
4.8
.
Then the above morphism is exact and hence we obtain an induced morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}))
.
Again by adjunction we obtain an exact morphism
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}))
which yields a canonical morphism
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}),\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S}^{\prime\prime}))
.
So we have the following diagram

\hbox to264.02pt{\vbox to90.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 132.007pt\lower-45.33925pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Since the essential image of the inclusion
\tau_{\leq 0}{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{C})
is extension-closed, the conclusion follows immediately.
∎

Corollary 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be small connective exact dg categories.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small exact dg category.
Then we have the following natural bijection

\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})).

Proof.

The required bijection follows from the following composition of bijections

\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{biexact}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{A},\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}))

where
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{exact}}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})
denotes the set of biexact morphisms.
∎

Let
\mathrm{Hqe}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathrm{ex}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}
consisting of connective exact dg categories.
Let
\mathcal{I}
be the category of free
k
-modules of finite rank which we consider as a dg category concentrated in degree zero.
We endow
\mathcal{I}
with the trivial exact structure.
It is clear that the tensor product
\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
extends to a bifunctor
-\boxtimes-:\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\times\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.

Proposition 4.11
.

The bifunctor
-\boxtimes-
defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Hqe}^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathrm{ex}}
with
\mathcal{I}
being the tensor unit.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
be small connective exact dg categories.
The tensor products
\mathcal{A}\boxtimes(\mathcal{B}\boxtimes\mathcal{C})
and
(\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B})\boxtimes\mathcal{C}
are both solutions of universal
triexact
morphisms from
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{C}
to an exact dg category.
Therefore they are naturally isomorphic in
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.
It is straightforward to check that this defines a monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.

We have
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{A}
in
\mathrm{Hqe}
which induces a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{B}\boxtimes\mathcal{A}

in
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\mathrm{ex}}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.
Therefore the monoidal structure is symmetric.

We may assume that the small connective exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
is such that
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is additive.
We define a morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}

G:\mathcal{I}\otimes\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A},\;\;(\bigoplus_{\text{$J$ finite}}k,A)\mapsto\bigoplus_{\text{$J$ finite}}A.

By
[
9
, Proposition 4.9 c)]
, it is biexact.
Let
F:\mathcal{I}\otimes\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
be the universal biexact morphism.
Then we have
F(\bigoplus_{\text{$J$ finite}}k,A)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}F(k,\bigoplus_{\text{$J$ finite}}A)
.
Hence, the morphism
G
is universal biexact. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}
is the tensor unit.
∎

By Corollary
4.10
, this monoidal structure is
closed
with internal Hom
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(?,-)
.

Corollary 4.12
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
be small connective exact dg categories.
We have the following quasi-equivalence of exact dg categories

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}\boxtimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{\mathrm{dg}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})).

5.
Application to
0
-Auslander extriangulated categories

An extriangulated category
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
is
0
-Auslander

[
16
]
if

•

it has enough projectives;

•

it has positive global dimension at most 1;

•

it has dominant dimension at least 1.

It is
reduced
if moreover the only projective-injectives are the zeros.
An exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
0
-Auslander
if
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is
0
-Auslander.
For a pretriangulated category
\mathcal{T}
with full dg subcategories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, we write
\mathcal{C}\ast\mathcal{D}
the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{T}
consisting of objects in the subcategory

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathcal{C})\ast H^{0}(\mathcal{D})=\{X\in H^{0}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle\mid\text{$\exists$ triangle $C\rightarrow X\rightarrow D\rightarrow\Sigma C$ in $H^{0}(\mathcal{T})$, where}

\displaystyle\text{ $C\in H^{0}(\mathcal{C})$, $D\in H^{0}(\mathcal{D})$}\}

of the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{T})
.
Inspired by the talk by Matthew Pressland on the recent work of
Fang–Gorsky–Palu–Plamondon–Pressland
[
35
]
, we have the following
0
-Auslander correspondence.

Theorem 5.1

(0-Auslander correspondence)
.

There is a bijective correspondence between the following:

(1)

equivalence classes of connective exact dg categories
\mathcal{A}
which are
0
-Auslander;

(2)

equivalence classes of pairs
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
consisting of

\bullet
a connective additive dg category
\mathcal{P}
, and

\bullet
an additive dg subcategory
\mathcal{I}
such that
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})
is covariantly finite in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
.

The bijection from
(1)
to
(2)
sends
\mathcal{A}
to the pair
(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}})
formed by the full dg subcategory
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}}
on the projectives of
\mathcal{A}
and its full dg subcategory
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}}
of projective-injectives.
The inverse bijection sends
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
to the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of the dg subcategory
(\mathcal{P}*\Sigma\mathcal{P})\cap\mathrm{ker}\,\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(-,\mathcal{I})
of
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
.

We will prove the theorem after Proposition
5.6
.
The map from (2) to (1) is obtained independently in
[
13
, Theorem 4.1]
.
Clearly, under the correspondence of the theorem,

a)

the exact dg categories in
(1)
whose corresponding extriangulated category is moreover reduced correspond to the pairs in
(2)
where
\mathcal{I}=0
;

b)

the exact dg categories in
(1)
with the trivial exact structure (so that each object is both projective and injective) correspond to the pairs in
(2)
where
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{P}
.

For a pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
in
(2)
, we denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
the dg subcategory
\mathcal{P}*\Sigma\mathcal{P}\cap\mathrm{ker}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(-,\mathcal{I}))
of
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
and by
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
) be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
whose objects are the direct summands in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
of objects in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})\subset H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
(resp.,
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})\subset H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
).
Clearly
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is stable under kernels of retractions in
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Below we will show that
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is
weakly idempotent complete
,
i.e., retractions of epimorphisms possess kernels, cf. Lemma
5.4
.

For a connective exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
the full dg subcategory
of
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
consisting of objects in the closure under kernels of retractions of
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
Clearly
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
is stable under extensions in
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
the thus inherited exact structure
\overline{\mathcal{S}}
induces a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{S}})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

Definition 5.2
.

Two connective exact dg categories
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
are equivalent
if there is an exact quasi-equivalence
\overline{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\overline{\mathcal{B}}
.

Two pairs
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
and
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{I}^{\prime})
in Theorem
5.1
(2) are equivalent if there is a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
inducing a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}
.

It is straightforward to verify the following fact.

Lemma 5.3
.

A connective exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is
0
-Auslander if and only if
(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
is
0
-Auslander.

Lemma 5.4
.

For a pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
in Theorem
5.1
(2), the category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is weakly idempotent complete.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}
is stable under kernels of retractions in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
.
Suppose we have
Y\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}X\oplus Z
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
, where both
Y
and
Z
are in
\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}
.
Then
X
is an extension of
\Sigma^{-1}Z
and
Y
and so
X\in\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{P}\ast\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}
.
Since
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(\Sigma^{-1}\mathcal{P},X)=0
, we have
X\in\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}
.
∎

Now we show that for a pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
in Theorem
5.1
(2), the dg subcategory
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
of
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
inherits a canonical exact dg structure whose corresponding extriangulated category is 0-Auslander.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a connective additive dg category and
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
a full additive dg subcategory such that
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})
is covariantly finite in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
.
The following statements hold.

(1)

The full subcategory
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
of
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
is extension-closed and the inherited extriangulated structure is 0-Auslander.
Put
\mathcal{A}=\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
and
\mathcal{S}
the corresponding exact structure on
\mathcal{A}
.

(2)

We have that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is the full dg subcategory of projectives in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
and
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is the full dg subcategory of projective-injectives in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
. Moreover, the pair
(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}},\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is equivalent to
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
.

(3)

The bounded dg derived category of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
.

Proof.

(1) Since
\mathcal{P}
is connective, the full dg subcqtegory
\mathcal{P}\ast\Sigma\mathcal{P}
is extension-closed in
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
and hence
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is also extension-closed in
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
.

(2) It is clear that the objects in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
are projective.
Let
X
be an object in
\mathcal{A}
.
Then we have the following triangle in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
:

(5.5.1)

P_{1}\xrightarrow{f}P_{2}\xrightarrow{g}X\xrightarrow{h}\Sigma P_{1}.

Suppose that
X
is projective in
\mathcal{A}
.
Then triangle (
5.5.1
) splits
and hence
X\oplus P_{1}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}P_{2}
and
X\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is the full dg subcategory of projective objects.
It is also clear that the objects in
\mathcal{I}
are projective-injective.
Let us show that
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
has dominant dimension at least
1
: if
X
is projective in
\mathcal{A}
, then the triangle (
5.5.1
) splits.
Since
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})
is covariantly finite in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
, we have a triangle

P_{2}\xrightarrow{u}I\xrightarrow{v}Y\xrightarrow{w}\Sigma X
in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
,
where
I\in\mathcal{I}
and
u
is a left
\mathcal{I}
-approximation of
P_{2}
.
Then it is clear that
Y
lies in
\mathcal{A}
.
We have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(A,\Sigma^{2}A^{\prime})=0
for
A
and
A^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
.
By definition, we have that
I
is injective in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
Therefore
Y
is injective in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
We have the following diagram in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})
:

(5.5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma P_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma P_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

Since
U
is an extension of
P_{1}
and
Y
, it is injective in
\mathcal{A}
.
Therefore, from the middle column of diagram (
5.5.2
) we deduce that the dominant dimension of
X
is a least 1.
Therefore,
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is a
0
-Auslander extriangulated category.
If
X
is moreover injective in
\mathcal{A}
, then the triangle in the middle column splits and hence
X
lies in
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{I}}
.
So
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
is the full dg subcategory of projective-injectives in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
.
It is straightforward to check that the pair
(\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}},\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is equivalent to
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
.

(3) The natural dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}\rightarrow\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
is clearly exact.
So we have the following diagram in
\mathrm{Hqe}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}).}

Note that the objects in
\mathcal{P}
are projective in
\mathcal{A}
and generate
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
as a triangulated category.
The claim follows from Theorem
3.1
and Proposition
3.17
.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
be a connective exact dg category such that the corresponding extriangulated category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is
0
-Auslander.
Let
\mathcal{P}\subset\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the full dg subcategory of projectives and

\mathcal{I}\subset\mathcal{P}
the full dg subcategory of projective-injectives (so
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})
is covariantly finite in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
).
Then
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is equivalent (in the sense of Definition
5.2
) to
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
where
\mathcal{A}=\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
and
\mathcal{S}
is the exact structure given by Lemma
5.5
.
When
\mathcal{I}=0
, then
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is exactly quasi-equivalent to
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.

Proof.

Since
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
is
0
-Auslander, the subcategory
H^{0}(\mathcal{I})
is covariantly finite in
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
.
So
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
forms a pair in Theorem
5.1
(2).
We have the following diagram in
\mathrm{Hqe}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

By Theorem
3.1
and Proposition
3.17
, the morphism
\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is quasi-fully faithful and
\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is generated as a triangulated category by the objects in
\mathcal{P}
.
Therefore, the morphism
\mu:\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
is a quasi-equivalence.
By Lemma
5.5
3),
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
is the dg derived category of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
.
It is enough to show that the quasi-essential image of
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
under the morphism
\mu
is in
\overline{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
.
Let
X\in\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})
be an object with a triangle (
5.5.1
) such that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}^{b}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}^{\prime})}(X,\Sigma\mathcal{I})=0
.
We claim that
X\in\overline{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
.
Indeed, the object
P_{1}
in the triangle (
5.5.1
) admits a triangle

P_{1}\rightarrow I\rightarrow K\rightarrow\Sigma P_{1}

where
I\in\mathcal{I}
and
K
is injective in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
.
We have the following diagram

(5.6.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma P_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

By assumption, we have
\mathrm{Hom}(X,\Sigma I)=0
and therefore
X\oplus I
is isomorphic to
U
, an extension of
P_{2}
and
K
(so
U\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
).
Hence
X
lies in
\overline{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
.
On the other hand, it is clear that each object in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is in the quasi-essential image of
\mathcal{A}
under the morphism
\mu
.
If
\mathcal{I}=0
, then
X\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}U
lies in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and hence
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is quasi-equivalent to
\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

By Lemma
5.3
, the property of being 0-Auslander is invariant under the equivalence relation introduced in Definition
5.2
.
For a connective 0-Auslander exact dg category
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
, the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{A}}
induces a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
and
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is quasi-equivalent to
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}})
.
Therefore, the map sending the equivalence class of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
to the equivalence class of
(\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{A}})
is well-defined.

Let
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
be a pair in Theorem
5.1
(2).
Put
\mathcal{A}=\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}}
.
By Lemma
5.5
,
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is a
0
-Auslander exact dg category.
By Lemma
5.4
we have that
H^{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I}})
is weakly idempotent complete.
Therefore, the map sending the equivalence class of a pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{I})
to the equivalence class of
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
is well-defined.

Combining Lemma
5.5
and Proposition
5.6
, we see that the above maps are inverse to each other.
∎

For an extriangulated category
(\mathcal{C},\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
,
we denote by
\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}
respectively
\mathrm{inj}\,\mathcal{C}
its full subcategory of projectives respectively injectives.
Inspired by recent results of Fang–Gorsky–Palu–Plamondon–Pressland
[
13
]
, we have the following result which relates algebraic 0-Auslander extriangulated categories to homotopy categories of two-term complexes.
It confirms a conjecture in
[
13
]
in the algebraic case.

Theorem 5.7
.

For each algebraic
0
-Auslander extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}
, we have
an equivalence of extriangulated categories

(5.7.1)

\mathcal{C}/[\mathrm{inj}\,\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}]\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{H}^{[-1,0]}(\mathrm{proj}\,\mathcal{C}/[\mathcal{I}])

where
\mathcal{I}
is the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
consisting of projective-injectives.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
be a connective dg enhancement for
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{I}
its full dg subcategory of projective-injectives.
Let
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{I}
be the dg quotient.
By Theorem
3.23
the dg quotient
\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{I}
carries a canonical exact structure
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{I},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
whose corresponding extriangulated category is
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})/[\mathcal{I}]
which is again 0-Auslander.
So we may replace
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
by
(\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{I},\overline{\mathcal{S}})
and assume
\mathcal{I}=0
.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be the full dg subcategory of projectives in
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{J}
the full dg subcategory of injectives in
\mathcal{A}
.
Then we have
\mathcal{J}=\Sigma\mathcal{P}
in
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{P})
and
\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{J})=0
.
By Proposition
5.6
,
\mathcal{A}
is exactly quasi-equivalent to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},0}
.
We keep the same notations
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{J}
for the corresponding subcategories
H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
and
H^{0}(\mathcal{J})
of
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.

The dg functor
\mathcal{P}\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{P})
induces a canonical exact morphism

\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},0}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{P}),0}

which is quasi-dense.
By taking 0-homology on both sides, we obtain a canonical functor

H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{[-1,0]}(H^{0}(\mathcal{P}))
.
In the homotopy category
\mathcal{H}^{[-1,0]}(H^{0}(\mathcal{P}))
of two-term complexes we have
\mathrm{Hom}(\Sigma\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P})=0
.
Therefore, it induces a canonical functor

F:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=H^{0}(\mathcal{A})/(\mathcal{J}\rightarrow\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{H}^{[-1,0]}(H^{0}(\mathcal{P}))=\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}.

We denote by
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
respectively
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}
the bifunctor associated with the extriangulated category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
respectively
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
Let
X
and
Y
be objects in
\mathcal{A}
.
Then we have triangles in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})

(5.7.2)

P_{1}\xrightarrow{f}P_{0}\rightarrow X\rightarrow\Sigma P_{1},

and

(5.7.3)

Q_{1}\xrightarrow{g}Q_{0}\rightarrow Y\rightarrow\Sigma Q_{1},

where
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are in
\mathcal{P}
for
i=0
,
1
.
Since
\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{J})=0
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}),

and

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{P},\Sigma\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(\mathcal{P},\Sigma\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(\mathcal{P},\Sigma\mathcal{P})=0.

We apply the cohomological functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})}(-,\mathcal{P})
to the triangle (
5.7.2
) and we get a long exact sequence of Hom spaces in
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{P})

\ldots\rightarrow(\Sigma P_{1},\mathcal{P})\rightarrow(X,\mathcal{P})\rightarrow(P_{0},\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{(f,\mathcal{P})}(P_{1},\mathcal{P})\rightarrow(X,\Sigma\mathcal{P})\rightarrow 0.

By direct inspection we see that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,\mathcal{P})
is isomorphic to the kernel of the map
\mathrm{Hom}(f,\mathcal{P})
and that
\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(X,\Sigma\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,\Sigma\mathcal{P})
. Therefore, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(FX,F\mathcal{P})

and
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,\Sigma\mathcal{P})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(FX,F\Sigma\mathcal{P})
.
Now from the
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
-triangle given by (
5.7.3
), we obtain the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,Q_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime}(X,Q_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime}(X,Q_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(FX,FQ_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(FX,FY)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}(FX,FQ_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}(FX,FQ_{0}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime}{(}X{,}g{)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}{(}FX{,}Fg{)}}

Since
(\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathbb{E}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
is hereditary, the space
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}(X,Y)
is isomorphic to the cokernel of
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}(X,g)
.
Similarly, the space
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}(FX,FY)
is isomorphic to the cokernel of
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}(FX,Fg)
.
We have
\mathbb{E}(X,Q_{1})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{H^{0}(\mathcal{A})}(X,\Sigma Q_{1})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,\Sigma Q_{1})
.
By the Five-Lemma, we see that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}}(FX,FY)
and
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}(FX,FY)
.
Therefore, the functor
F
is fully faithful and is an equivalence of extriangulated categories.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

While the statement of Theorem
5.7
is irrelevant with the dg enhancement, our construction of the equivalence (
5.7.1
) does depend on the dg enhancement.
